5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file; `bunx biome check` flagged one `noArrayIndexKey` on the highlight-splitting `.map` (pre-existing tolerated pattern elsewhere in this app, documented in earlier entries above — a static per-render split array, not a reordering list) and the same pre-existing `useExhaustiveDependencies` note on the mount-time keyword-suggestion effect (unchanged, already had a deliberate `eslint-disable` comment) — neither is a new regression. Added a Changelog entry (v2.21.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### ANTHROPIC_KEY rename; Knowledge Base true semantic search; Dashboard Knowledge Base card

1. **Goal / behavior**: (a) The Anthropic option (AI Summariser, Data Explorer, Insights, Knowledge Base, AI Chat) now looks for a secret named `ANTHROPIC_KEY` instead of `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` — purely a rename, no functional change while the secret remains unset (OpenAI still works as the default/fallback). (b) The Knowledge Base page gained a **Semantic search** toggle (on by default): when on, the search query is embedded and ranked against every stored document's own embedding by cosine similarity — true meaning-based matching — instead of the original exact-keyword `ILIKE` search; each result shows a "% match" badge in this mode. (c) The Dashboard gained a 5th summary card — Knowledge Base document count, total chunk count, and last-updated time — linking to `/knowledge-base`.
2. **Data flow**: (a) `aiModelService.ts`'s `callAnthropic` now reads `process.env.ANTHROPIC_KEY` (error message updated to match); `aiProviders.ts`'s doc comment updated too. (b) New `embedSearchQuery` server function in `aiModelService.ts` calls `https://api.openai.com/v1/embeddings` (`text-embedding-3-small`, using `OPENAI_API_KEY` regardless of the selected chat provider — Anthropic has no public embeddings endpoint) — verified live to return 1536-dimension vectors, matching `vector_dims(embedding)` confirmed live against the connector's actual stored column. New `useEmbedSearchQuery` hook wraps it. `knowledge-base.tsx` — new `buildSemanticUnionQuery(tables, embedding, limit)` builds a `UNION ALL` query using pgvector's `<=>` cosine-distance operator (verified live: a self-distance query returned exactly `0`, and a deliberately dimension-mismatched test vector correctly errored, confirming the operator/casts parse as expected) ordered by distance ascending; `VectorRow` gained an optional `distance`, `DocumentGroup` gained `bestDistance` (the minimum distance across a document's chunks, computed in `groupResultsByDocument`), and a `semanticSearch` toggle state routes `handleSearch` through `embedQuery.mutate` → `buildSemanticUnionQuery` when on (and an empty query always falls back to the plain keyword browse-all query, since there's nothing to embed). The "% match" badge computes `(1 - distance / 2) * 100` (cosine distance ranges 0-2 for normalized vectors). (c) `index.tsx` — new `kbStats`/`kbLoading`/`kbError` state populated by a `useEffect`-driven `useQuerySource()` call against the same connector/tables the Knowledge Base page uses, running a single `COUNT(*)`/`COUNT(DISTINCT metadata::jsonb->>'filename')`/`MAX(created_at)` query (verified live, returned `chunk_count: 22, doc_count: 3` against the real data) — a new 5th card (grid widened from `lg:grid-cols-4` to `lg:grid-cols-5`) renders it, wrapped in a `Link` to `/knowledge-base`.
3. **Inputs/config**: `ANTHROPIC_KEY` is still not set in this environment (same as `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` before it) — this change only relabels which secret name is expected, it does not add the secret itself. `OPENAI_API_KEY` (used for both chat completions and now embeddings) is unchanged and already present.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Semantic search costs one extra OpenAI API call (the embedding) per search beyond the existing chat-completion calls — acceptable given search is user-initiated, not automatic/continuous. The embedding model is hardcoded to `text-embedding-3-small`/1536-dim to match what's already stored in the vector store's `embedding` column; if the underlying documents were ever re-embedded with a different model/dimension, this query would need updating to match (a mismatch would surface as a clear Postgres "different vector dimensions" error, not a silent wrong answer — confirmed via that exact live test). The Dashboard's Knowledge Base card is a read-only count/stat — it does not let the user search or browse documents inline, only links out to the full page.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified every new piece of SQL/API behavior directly against the real connector/API before wiring it into the app: `vector_dims(embedding)` (1536), OpenAI embeddings response dimension (1536, matching), a self-distance `<=>` query (returned `0`), a dimension-mismatch `<=>` query (correctly errored rather than silently succeeding), and the Dashboard's summary query (`chunk_count: 22, doc_count: 3` against real data). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on every touched/created file. Added three Changelog entries (v2.22.0, v2.23.0, v2.24.0) and three To-Do items, all marked done.

### Fix: Knowledge Base results shown as plain unformatted text

1. **Goal / behavior**: Knowledge Base search results no longer render as one flat, unstructured block of text — headers, sub-headers, bullet/numbered lists, bold text, tables, and code spans inside a matched document now actually render as that formatting instead of showing as literal characters.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/query change. `knowledge-base.tsx` — the per-document result preview switched from a plain `<p>{highlightMatches(shownText, search)}</p>` to the shared `<Markdown>` component (`@/components/ui/markdown`, same one already used for the page's "Ask AI" answer) with a `className` of prose-style Tailwind arbitrary-selector rules (headings, lists, tables, code, blockquote) matching the pattern used by `ChatMarkdown.tsx`/`insights.tsx`. `highlightMatches(text, term)` was changed from returning an array of React nodes (`<mark>` elements) to returning a plain string with matches wrapped in markdown bold (`**term**`), since its output now feeds into a markdown renderer instead of being rendered as raw JSX children; a `[&_strong]:bg-primary/20` style makes the resulting bold text look like the previous `<mark>` highlight.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Highlighting now piggybacks on markdown bold syntax — if a document's own real bold text coincides exactly with the search term it renders indistinguishably from a highlight (a pre-existing acceptable tradeoff for reusing the shared renderer rather than a custom highlight-aware markdown parser). Documents whose source text has no markdown syntax at all render unchanged (plain paragraphs), only documents that actually contain headers/lists/bold see a visible difference.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.26.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: Knowledge Base headings still not showing after Markdown switch

1. **Goal / behavior**: The previous "render results as Markdown" fix (entry above) had no visible effect, because the vector store's stored `text` is raw flattened plain text with **no newlines or markdown syntax at all** — even documents that look structured (numbered sections, an "Introduction"/"Conclusion") lost that structure entirely during ingestion. Result cards now reconstruct a reasonable approximation of that structure before handing text to the Markdown renderer, so headings genuinely appear.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/query change. `knowledge-base.tsx` — new `reconstructHeadings(text)` heuristic: scans word-by-word for `Section \d+:` followed by up to 4 capitalized words (used as a `##` heading), and for standalone heading words (`Introduction`, `Conclusion`, `Summary`, `Background`, `Overview`) appearing on their own, inserting blank lines + `## ` markdown syntax around each match. Applied to `shownText` before `highlightMatches` in the result card's `<Markdown>` block.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Verified live against the actual stored `lightning_embedding` rows via `aisquared_connectors_query_source` — confirmed the raw text has zero `\n` characters and no `#`/`*` markdown syntax, which is why the earlier Markdown-only fix was a no-op.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This is a plain-English heuristic, not a real document-structure parser — it can occasionally pull one extra word from the start of the next sentence into a "Section N:" heading (e.g. "Section 1: Technology Overview Machine" instead of stopping at "Overview"), since there's no punctuation boundary between a title and the following sentence in the raw text. Only `Section N:` and the five listed standalone words are recognized; other heading conventions (ALL CAPS lines, numbered outlines without "Section", etc.) won't be reconstructed. Documents with no such cues at all render unchanged (plain flowing paragraphs), which is correct since there's nothing to reconstruct.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified the raw stored text (no newlines/markdown) via `aisquared_connectors_query_source` before concluding the earlier fix needed this follow-up. Node-tested `reconstructHeadings` directly against the real sample document text and confirmed "Introduction" and both "Section N:" occurrences correctly became `##` headings. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.27.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Knowledge Base improvements: filters, sorting, snippets, citations, follow-ups

1. **Goal / behavior**: Seven improvements to `/knowledge-base`. (a) Better heading reconstruction — `Section N:` titles no longer swallow one extra word from the following sentence. (b) A **Document** filter (dropdown of known filenames) and **From/To date** filters, applying to both keyword and semantic search. (c) Results default to a **Google-style snippet** centered on the matching text instead of always showing the first 600 characters. (d) A **Sort by** control: Relevance / Newest first / Oldest first / Alphabetical. (e) Each result card gained an **"Add to Ask AI"** toggle so the user can pick exactly which documents ground the AI's answer, instead of always the top 5 by search order. (f) AI-answer citations like `[doc 1]` are now clickable links that scroll to the matching result card. (g) "Ask AI" is now a running **conversation** supporting follow-up questions (with a "New conversation" reset), instead of one question replacing the previous answer.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `knowledge-base.tsx` — (a) `reconstructHeadings`'s word-lookahead was inverted to the correct rule: a capitalized word is only added to a `Section N:` title if the word *after* it is also capitalized (still inside the multi-word title phrase); the first capitalized word whose follower is lowercase is the next sentence's subject and is excluded, fixing the previous off-by-one-word overreach. (b) New `buildFilterConditions(filenameFilter, dateFrom, dateTo)` returns shared SQL condition strings consumed by both `buildUnionQuery`'s `WHERE` and a new `extraConditions` param on `buildSemanticUnionQuery`; new `extractFilenames(rows)` (same on-mount sample query already used for keyword suggestions) populates the Document dropdown. (c) New `buildSnippet(text, term)` finds the first case-insensitive occurrence of the search term and returns a ~460-character window centered on it (with ellipses), falling back to the original first-600-chars behavior when there's no term or no match; the "Show full document" toggle's "is there more to show" check now compares the snippet's length to the full text's length instead of a fixed threshold. (d) New `sortDocumentGroups(groups, sortBy)` plus a `DocumentGroup.createdAt` field (max `created_at` across a document's merged chunks) backing Newest/Oldest; Alphabetical sorts by filename; Relevance re-sorts by `bestDistance` only when semantic search actually produced distances, otherwise leaves the SQL-provided order. (e) New `contextDocKeys: Set<string>` state toggled per-card; `handleAsk` uses the selected set (capped at `MAX_CONTEXT_DOCUMENTS`) when non-empty, else falls back to the previous top-N-by-search-order behavior. (f) New `docAnchorId(key)` gives every result card a stable HTML `id`; new `linkCitations(text, keys)` rewrites `[doc N]` into a markdown link `[...](#id)` using the *specific* ordered list of document keys that were actually sent as context for that answer (stored per assistant turn, since the selected context can change between questions in the same conversation). (g) `question`/`answer` state was replaced with `conversation: ConversationTurn[]` (`{role:"user", content}` or `{role:"assistant", content, citationKeys}`); `handleAsk` now sends the full prior transcript plus the new question in the `messages` array to `useAiModel()` (which already supports multi-turn `role`/`content` messages) so follow-ups have context, and appends both the user's question and the model's reply to `conversation` rather than overwriting a single `answer` string.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Reuses the existing confirmed vector-store connector (id `1142`) and the existing `embedSearchQuery`/`callAiModel` server functions — no new endpoints.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The Document filter's dropdown is populated from the same 50-row on-mount sample used for keyword suggestions — a filename only present outside that sample won't appear as a filter option (though it would still be found by search itself if searched directly; this only affects the dropdown's list, not what's searchable). Date filters compare against the chunk-level `created_at` column, not a document-level ingestion date. The heading-reconstruction fix still only recognizes `Section N:` and the five standalone heading words (documented in the earlier entry above) — this change only corrects word-boundary precision, not the coverage. Ask AI's conversation history is not persisted (component state only) — refreshing the page or running a new search clears it. Citation links only work for `[doc N]` references that match a real index into that specific answer's `citationKeys` — a model that fabricates a citation number outside that range leaves the bracketed text unlinked (falls back to plain text, not a broken link).
5. **Validation**: Node-tested the corrected `reconstructHeadings` word-lookahead against the real sample document text (both `Section 1:`/`Section 2:` titles now stop at the exact right word). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.28.0) and a To-Do item documenting all seven changes, marked done.

### New page: Document Intelligence Pipeline (`/document-pipeline`)

1. **Goal / behavior**: A new page (Files & Media nav group) where an uploaded document runs through a configurable, multi-stage AI pipeline: **Extract text** -> **Summarize** -> **Extract tags/entities** -> **Classify document type** -> **Generate report**. Stages can be toggled on/off per run and saved as a named, reusable preset (with a default AI provider). Each job shows live per-stage status (pending/running/done/failed) in a detail dialog; a failed stage can be **retried individually** without re-running the whole pipeline; once the report stage completes, the generated Markdown report can be downloaded from Storage.
2. **Data flow**: New tables in `src/server/db/schema.ts` — `pipeline_configs` (name, ordered `stages` text array, `provider`), `document_jobs` (source `storage_file_id` FK, snapshot `stages` array, `provider`, overall `status`, optional `report_storage_file_id` FK), `pipeline_stage_results` (`job_id` FK cascade, `stage`, `stage_order`, `status`, `output`, `error_message`, timestamps) — migrated via `db:generate`/`db:migrate`. New `src/services/db/documentPipelineService.ts`: `listPipelineConfigs`/`createPipelineConfig`/`deletePipelineConfig`; `createDocumentJob` (inserts the job plus one `pending` stage-result row per selected stage); `listDocumentJobs` (joins in each job's stage rows and its source/report `storageFiles` rows); `runPipelineStage(jobId, stage)` — the core orchestrator: marks the stage `running`, and for `extract` downloads the source file's bytes from S3 (`downloadFile`) and asks the AI provider to clean/extract the text; for `summarize`/`tags`/`classify` all read the **`extract` stage's own output** (not the immediately-preceding stage) as their input, since they're independent analyses of the same extracted text rather than a chain; for `report` combines every completed stage's output into one AI-generated Markdown report, uploads it to `exports/document-pipeline/` via `uploadFile`, and records the new `storage_files` row's id as `reportStorageFileId` on the job. On success marks the stage `done` and recomputes the job's overall status (`done` if every stage is done, `failed` if any stage is `failed`, else `running`); on error marks just that stage `failed` with the error message and the job `failed`, leaving every other already-`done` stage's output intact for the "retry just this stage" flow. `aiModelService.ts` gained an exported plain `callAiText(provider, messages, instructions)` helper (extracted out of `callAiModel`'s handler) specifically so this service's own `createServerFn` handler can get raw model text without one server function calling another's HTTP boundary directly. `document-pipeline.tsx` — uploads via the existing `uploadStorageFile` (folder `uploads`), then `createDocumentJob`, then runs `runPipelineStage` sequentially client-side (`for` loop with `mutateAsync`, stopping at the first failure) since there's no background job runner in this template; a job's detail dialog shows every stage's status/output and a per-stage Retry button that just re-runs `runPipelineStage` for that one stage.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL`, the provisioned S3 storage, and the existing `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_KEY` secrets via `useAiModel`'s underlying `aiModelService`. **Only plain-text file formats are accepted for upload** (`.txt`, `.md`, `.csv`, `.json`, enforced both by the file input's `accept` attribute and a client-side extension check) — this app has no PDF/DOCX parsing library installed and none could be added, so a binary file's raw bytes can't be turned into real extracted text; accepting only formats that already *are* text avoids silently producing garbage output.
4. **Constraints or known limitations**: Pipeline execution is synchronous request/response per stage, orchestrated by the browser tab making sequential calls — there's no server-side background queue, so closing the tab mid-run leaves the job at whatever stage last completed (resumable later via the same per-stage Retry button, but not automatically resumed). Retrying a stage does not cascade — retrying a fixed "extract" does NOT automatically re-run "summarize"/"tags"/"classify" even though their input changed; the user must retry those too if they depend on the corrected output (this was an explicit, confirmed tradeoff, not an oversight). The `report` stage truncates the extracted-text section to 4,000 characters and every AI call truncates its input to 12,000 characters (`MAX_STAGE_INPUT_CHARS`) to keep prompts bounded — a very long document's tail wouldn't be seen by any stage.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `pipeline_configs`, `document_jobs`, `pipeline_stage_results` in the table list). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). **Live-verified the actual pipeline logic end-to-end** with a real test invoice file: uploaded to S3, ran `extract`/`summarize`/`classify` through the real OpenAI API via the exact same prompts the service uses — all three stages produced correct output (extract preserved the invoice fields, summary was accurate, classification correctly identified it as "Invoice") — then cleaned up the test rows/file. `bunx biome check`/`format --write` on all touched/created files (fixed one real `noLabelWithoutControl` lint error by giving the stage checkbox an explicit `id`/`htmlFor` pair instead of an implicit wrapping `<label>`). Confirmed `/document-pipeline` returns 200 from the dev server and `routeTree.gen.ts` picked up the new route. Added a Changelog entry (v2.29.0) and a To-Do item documenting the new page, marked done.

### Document Pipeline: allow PDF/DOCX/ODT/RTF uploads via a hand-rolled extractor

1. **Goal / behavior**: The Document Intelligence Pipeline's upload button now accepts `.pdf`, `.docx`, `.odt`, and `.rtf` in addition to the original plain-text formats (`.txt`, `.md`, `.csv`, `.json`). Uploading one of these runs the pipeline exactly as before, with the "Extract text" stage now pulling real text out of the binary/XML format instead of requiring plain text.
2. **Data flow**: New `src/services/db/documentTextExtraction.ts` — since this app has no PDF/Office parsing library installed and none can be added, this hand-rolls just enough of each format using only Node's built-in `zlib` (both formats commonly use DEFLATE compression, which `zlib` supports natively): `extractPdfText(bytes, zlib)` regex-scans the raw PDF bytes for `<< ... >> stream ... endstream` blocks, inflates any `/FlateDecode` ones (or takes uncompressed ones as-is), and extracts text from `Tj`/`TJ` content-stream operators (with PDF string-escape unescaping); a minimal hand-written ZIP reader (`findEndOfCentralDirectory`/`readCentralDirectory`/`readZipEntryData`, using `zlib.inflateRawSync` for compressed entries) powers `extractDocxText` (reads `word/document.xml`, splits on `</w:p>`, pulls `<w:t>` runs) and `extractOdtText` (reads `content.xml`, splits on `</text:p>`, strips tags) — both formats are just ZIP archives of XML under the hood. `.rtf` needs no special parser (it's already plain ASCII with control words) and is decoded as text like the original formats, relying on the existing AI "extract and clean" prompt to strip RTF control codes. The top-level `extractRawText(bytes, fileName)` dispatches by extension and throws a clear, specific error (rather than returning garbled binary as if it were real text) when the extracted text comes back under 20 characters for a non-plain-text format — signaling a scanned/image-only PDF, encryption, or an unsupported font encoding. `documentPipelineService.ts`'s `runPipelineStage`'s `extract` branch now calls `extractRawText(bytes, job.fileName)` instead of a raw `TextDecoder` UTF-8 decode; `ACCEPTED_FILE_EXTENSIONS` grew to include the four new formats.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config, no new dependency (deliberately — only `node:zlib`, dynamically imported inside the extraction functions per the project's server-import convention). Legacy binary `.doc` (pre-2007 Word, an OLE binary format, not a ZIP or plain text) is intentionally excluded from `ACCEPTED_FILE_EXTENSIONS` — supporting it would need a much larger binary-format parser.
4. **Constraints or known limitations**: PDF extraction is best-effort — it works well for PDFs with standard/simple text encoding but has no CMap/ToUnicode decoding (which a real PDF library would do), so PDFs with subsetted/custom font encodings (common from design tools), scanned/image-only pages, or encryption will yield little or no usable text (surfaced as the clear extraction error above, confirmed live, rather than silent garbage). DOCX/ODT extraction discards all formatting/images/table structure, returning plain paragraphs only. The ZIP reader only handles the two compression methods ZIP itself defines as "stored" (0) and "deflate" (8) — a DOCX/ODT saved with a different compression method (rare, but possible from some non-standard tools) would throw an "Unsupported ZIP compression method" error.
5. **Validation**: Hand-built real PDF (uncompressed content stream, valid xref table), DOCX (ZIP with a deflated `word/document.xml`), and ODT (ZIP with a deflated `content.xml`) test files from scratch (no generator tools available in this environment) and ran `extractPdfText`/`extractDocxText`/`extractOdtText`/`extractRawText` against all three directly — all four extracted the expected text correctly, including the too-short-text error path. Ran a full pipeline simulation (upload PDF to S3 -> `extractRawText` -> `callAiText` extract-cleanup -> `callAiText` classify) against a synthetic purchase-order PDF through the live OpenAI API — extraction, cleanup, and classification (correctly identified as "Contract") all succeeded; test rows/files cleaned up after. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome check`/`format --write` on all touched/created files (only an info-level `noUselessSwitchCase` suggestion remained, not an error). Confirmed `/document-pipeline` still returns 200 from the dev server and `bun run db:status` still lists all tables correctly. Added a Changelog entry (v2.30.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: PDF extraction failing on real-world PDFs (CID/hex-encoded text)

1. **Goal / behavior**: Uploading a normal PDF — anything exported from Word, Google Docs, or a browser's "Print to PDF", which is the vast majority of real-world PDFs — no longer fails the "Extract text" stage with "Couldn't extract readable text from this PDF file." The built-in extractor now handles the hex-encoded text these PDFs almost universally use, not just the rare literal-string case the initial version supported.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `documentTextExtraction.ts` — root cause: PDFs with embedded/subset fonts (the default for nearly every PDF generator) show text via hex string operands (`<48656C6C6F> Tj`, where each 2-byte pair is a font-specific glyph/CID code, not a readable character) rather than literal `(Hello) Tj` strings — the original extractor only matched literal strings, so these PDFs silently produced zero extracted text and hit the "too short" honesty-check error. Fixed by: (a) `collectPdfStreams(buf, zlib)` — factored out of `extractPdfText`, finds every stream in the file (both content streams and font CMap streams use the same `<< ... >> stream ... endstream` structure); (b) `parseToUnicodeCMap(text, map)` — parses a font's embedded `/ToUnicode` CMap stream's `beginbfchar`/`beginbfrange` blocks (the near-universal accessibility/text-extraction mapping PDF generators embed) into a CID -> Unicode-string `Map`, identifying CMap streams by their own content (presence of `beginbfchar`/`beginbfrange`) rather than by resolving `/ToUnicode N 0 R` indirect object references, since a full indirect-object resolver would be substantially more code for the same practical result; (c) `extractPdfText` now runs two passes over the collected streams — pass 1 builds one merged CID map from every CMap-like stream in the file, pass 2 extracts text from every stream that looks like a content stream (matches `/\bT[Jj]\b/`), decoding each text operand (literal or hex) via `decodePdfStringOperand`, which uses the CID map for hex operands when one exists (falling back to raw byte-as-Latin-1 decoding otherwise — the previous fallback behavior, kept for PDFs with no embedded CMap).
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config, still zero external dependencies (only `node:zlib`).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The CID map is merged from *every* font's CMap in the document into one global map — if two different fonts reuse the same CID number for different characters (uncommon but possible), the later-parsed font's mapping silently wins; an acceptable tradeoff for a best-effort extractor with no per-font text-run association. PDFs with genuinely no embedded `/ToUnicode` CMap and CID-encoded text (rare — usually only very old or deliberately-obfuscated PDFs) still can't be decoded correctly, since there'd be no mapping to decode glyph IDs into real characters at all; these still surface the same clear "couldn't extract" error rather than garbage. Scanned/image-only PDFs are still entirely unsupported (no OCR) — unchanged from the original entry above.
5. **Validation**: Hand-built a synthetic PDF that mimics real CID/Identity-H text encoding end-to-end — sequential fake glyph IDs in a hex `Tj` operand plus a matching embedded `/ToUnicode` CMap stream (`beginbfchar` block mapping each fake CID back to its real Unicode character) — and confirmed `extractPdfText` decoded it back to the exact original text. Re-ran the original literal-string-PDF test to confirm no regression (still extracts correctly without any CMap present). Re-ran the full `extractRawText(bytes, "doc.pdf")` path against the CID test PDF to confirm the fix reaches all the way through the public API the pipeline actually calls. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome check`/`format --write` on the touched file (fixed one real `useIterableCallbackReturn` lint error from a `forEach` with an implicit return; one pre-existing info-level `noUselessSwitchCase` suggestion left as-is, not an error). Confirmed `/document-pipeline` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.31.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Document Pipeline: faster runs (parallel stages), progress bar, request timeouts

1. **Goal / behavior**: Three improvements addressing "the pipeline is slow with no feedback": (a) `Summarize`/`Extract tags`/`Classify` now run **concurrently** instead of one-at-a-time (they each independently depend only on `Extract`'s output, not on each other), meaningfully cutting total wall-clock time. (b) A live progress bar + short status message ("Extracting text...", "Running 3 stages in parallel...", "Generating report...") now shows on every job row in the list and in the job detail dialog, instead of just a static "Running" badge. (c) Every AI request across the whole app (not just the pipeline) now has a 45-second timeout, so a stalled upstream call fails with a clear error instead of hanging forever with no feedback.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `document-pipeline.tsx` — `runStagesSequentially` (client-side orchestrator) now splits a job's selected stages into `extract` (always first, if selected), `independentStages` (`summarize`/`tags`/`classify`, run via `Promise.all(independentStages.map(stage => runStageMutation.mutateAsync(...)))`), and `report` (always last, if selected) — each phase's start sets a new `runningLabel` state string; a `Progress` component (`@/components/ui/progress`, plain `value` 0-100 prop) renders `doneCount/stages.length * 100` on both the job-list row (now restructured from a single row into a two-part column: header row + progress bar) and inside the detail dialog (an IIFE block computing the same percentage from whichever `jobs` query data is freshest). `aiModelService.ts` — new `fetchWithTimeout(url, init, timeoutMs = 45_000)` wraps `fetch` with an `AbortController`/`setTimeout`, converting an `AbortError` into a clear `"Request to <host> timed out after Ns."` error; all three of the file's raw `fetch` calls (Anthropic chat, OpenAI chat, OpenAI embeddings) now go through it, so every AI-powered page in the app (not just this one) is protected, not only the pipeline.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Timeout is a fixed 45s constant (`AI_REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`), not user-configurable.
4. **Constraints or known limitations**: If one of the 3 parallel stages fails, `Promise.all` rejects immediately and the client stops orchestrating further stages for that job — but the other in-flight parallel requests were already sent to the server and still complete and record their own result independently (matching the existing "retry just the failed stage" design, not a partial-parallel-failure bug). The 45s timeout is a single fixed value for every provider/call shape in the file (chat completions and embeddings alike) — a genuinely large document's `report` stage prompt could in principle need longer than 45s on a slow day, in which case it would need retrying via the existing per-stage Retry button rather than auto-extending. This change does not add server-side background execution — the pipeline is still orchestrated by sequential/parallel client `await`s in the browser tab (documented limitation from the original page entry above still applies: closing the tab mid-run pauses progress until manually resumed via Retry).
5. **Validation**: **Timeout** — tested `fetchWithTimeout` in isolation against a deliberately unresponsive local HTTP server (never responds) with a 1s timeout; confirmed it threw the expected clear error at ~1000ms, not hanging indefinitely. **Parallelization** — measured the real speedup by calling `callAiText("openai", ...)` for the summarize/tags/classify prompts sequentially vs. via `Promise.all` against the live OpenAI API: sequential took ~2815ms, parallel took ~1531ms (**1.84x** speedup), confirming the change has a real, measurable effect rather than just a theoretical one. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across both touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome check`/`format --write` on both touched files — no new lint issues. Confirmed `/document-pipeline` still returns 200 from the dev server and `bun run db:status` still lists all tables correctly. Added a Changelog entry (v2.32.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Document Pipeline: Stop buttons for upload/retry

1. **Goal / behavior**: A Stop button now appears in three places on `/document-pipeline`: next to the Upload button while a file is uploading, on a job's row while its pipeline is actively running, and next to a stage's Retry button while that specific retry is in flight. Clicking Stop cancels the request immediately rather than leaving the user stuck waiting with no way out.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `document-pipeline.tsx` — three `useRef<AbortController | null>` (`uploadAbortRef`, `runAbortRef`, `retryAbortRef`), one per cancellable operation. `uploadMutation`'s `mutationFn` was changed from a plain `(formData) => uploadStorageFile({ data: formData })` to `({ formData, signal }) => uploadStorageFile({ data: formData, signal })` so a signal can flow through; `createDocumentJob`/`runPipelineStage` calls already accept `signal` directly alongside `data` (confirmed from `@tanstack/start-client-core`'s `CompiledFetcherFnOptions` type — every `createServerFn`-generated client call accepts `{ data, signal }`, no wrapper needed for those two). `handleFileSelected` creates a controller, passes its signal to both the upload and job-creation calls, and a new `isUploadingFile` state (rather than the old `uploadMutation.isPending || createJobMutation.isPending` combo) drives the button/Stop-button visibility so it stays true across both awaited calls. `runStagesSequentially` creates a controller stored in `runAbortRef`, threads `signal` through every `runStageMutation.mutateAsync` call (including each of the parallel `Promise.all` calls), and adds an explicit `if (controller.signal.aborted) throw new DOMException("", "AbortError")` check between the extract/parallel/report phases so a stop mid-phase prevents the *next* phase from ever starting (the phase already in flight can't be un-sent, per the constraint below). `handleRetryStage` gained the same controller pattern plus a new `retryingStage: {jobId, stage} | null` state so the detail dialog can show a Stop button (and a spinner via `StatusBadge status="running"`) for the exact stage being retried, swapping back to the normal Retry button once it settles. All three catch blocks distinguish a user-initiated abort (`controller.signal.aborted`) from a real failure — abort shows `toast.info(...)` and skips the Error Log write; a genuine failure still shows `toast.error(...)` and logs as before.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints or known limitations**: This is cooperative/client-side cancellation, not true server-side job cancellation — there's no background job queue in this template to interrupt mid-execution. Aborting stops the client from *waiting* for and *starting further* stages immediately, and does cancel the underlying HTTP request the moment it's sent, but if the server had already started processing a stage (e.g. mid-AI-call) before the abort reached it, that stage may still complete and write its own `done`/`failed` result to the database in the background — the UI will simply reflect whatever that ends up being next time it's viewed, and the existing per-stage Retry button remains the way to resume/redo it either way.
5. **Validation**: Verified `createServerFn`'s generated client calls genuinely accept a cancellation `signal` by reading `@tanstack/start-client-core`'s own `CompiledFetcherFnOptions`/`ServerFnBaseOptions` type definitions before relying on it (not guessed). Live-tested that aborting actually cancels a real in-flight request — fired a real `fetch` to `https://api.openai.com/v1/chat/completions` (a large-essay prompt, deliberately slow) with an `AbortController`, called `.abort()` after 50ms, and confirmed the promise rejected with a genuine `AbortError` at ~83ms rather than waiting for the full response. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome check`/`format --write` on the touched file — fixed one real `noUnusedVariables` warning from an unused caught error binding. Confirmed `/document-pipeline` still returns 200 from the dev server and `bun run db:status` still lists all tables correctly. Added a Changelog entry (v2.33.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: Document Pipeline could be started in a guaranteed-to-fail stage combination

1. **Goal / behavior**: It's no longer possible to trigger `"Pipeline stopped: The \"Extract text\" stage must complete successfully before this stage can run."` from the UI. Root cause: `Summarize`/`Extract tags`/`Classify`/`Generate report` all read the `extract` stage's own output as their input (by design — see the original pipeline build entry above), but the stage-picker checkboxes let a user uncheck "Extract text" while leaving any of those other four checked, which is a combination `runPipelineStage` was always going to reject immediately.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `document-pipeline.tsx` — `toggleStage(stage)` now enforces the dependency directly: checking any stage other than `extract` auto-adds `extract` to the selection; unchecking `extract` while any other stage is still selected clears the entire selection (rather than leaving a combination that's guaranteed to fail) instead of just toggling the one checkbox. `applyPreset(presetId)` repairs the same issue for a preset that could have been saved before this fix existed — it adds `extract` back into a non-empty stage set on load. `handleFileSelected` also got a defensive `selectedStages.size > 1 && !selectedStages.has("extract")` guard with a clear toast, as belt-and-suspenders in case `selectedStages` is ever set some other way in the future. A small UI hint ("Required by the other selected stages.") appears under the Extract text option whenever more than one stage is selected.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: None new — this purely closes off an invalid selection path; the underlying stage-dependency design (documented in the original page entry) is unchanged.
5. **Validation**: Checked the live `pipeline_configs` table directly — zero saved presets exist, confirming the reported bug came from live checkbox interaction (unchecking Extract text) rather than a stored broken preset, and that no data repair was needed. Unit-tested the corrected `toggleStage` logic's invariants in isolation (unchecking extract while others are selected clears everything; checking a dependent stage alone auto-adds extract; unchecking that dependent stage again leaves extract selected on its own) — all passed. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome check`/`format --write` on the touched file — no new lint issues. Added a Changelog entry (v2.34.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: Document Pipeline retry could re-trigger the "Extract text" dependency error

1. **Goal / behavior**: Reported error — `Stage failed: The "Extract text" stage must complete successfully before this stage can run.` — could still happen when clicking **Retry** on a dependent stage (Summarize/Extract tags/Classify/Generate report) whose job's Extract text stage hadn't actually completed (e.g. Extract itself failed on that upload). The stage-selection checkboxes were already fixed against this in an earlier entry, but the per-stage Retry button in the job detail dialog had no equivalent guard. The Retry button for any non-extract stage is now disabled (with a tooltip and an inline hint under the error message) until that job's Extract text stage status is `"done"`.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `document-pipeline.tsx` — the stage list inside the job detail dialog now computes `extractDone` (whether the job's own `extract` stage row has `status === "done"`) once per render, and a `retryBlocked` flag per stage (`stage.stage !== "extract" && !extractDone`); when `retryBlocked` is true the Retry button renders `disabled` with a title tooltip, and the failed-stage error text is swapped for a clearer explanation ("must complete successfully before this stage can run... Retry Extract text first") instead of the raw server error.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This only blocks retrying a dependent stage from the UI before Extract is done — it doesn't auto-retry Extract for the user; they still click Extract's own Retry first, then the dependent stage's Retry becomes enabled once it succeeds.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.34.1) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Removed Weather page; merged Documents into Storage

1. **Goal / behavior**: Following a full-app page audit, two cleanup changes: (a) the Weather page (`/weather`, an unrelated public-API demo with no persistence and no connection to this app's own data) was deleted outright. (b) The Documents page (`/documents`, "Document Archive") was merged into the Storage page (`/storage`) — Storage now has a **Type filter** (All Files / Documents / Images) plus Documents' tagging and searchable-content features, so there's one file-management page instead of two overlapping ones (both previously listed/downloaded/deleted the same underlying `storage_files` + S3 objects).
2. **Data flow**: No schema change (Storage already used the same `storage_files` table and `tags`/`extractedText` columns Documents did, via `listAllStorageObjects()`, which already returns those fields). `src/routes/_protected/storage.tsx` gained: a `typeFilter` state (`"all" | "documents" | "images"`, filtering on `contentType?.startsWith("image/")`), the search box now also matches `tags`/`extractedText` (not just filename), a `Tag` icon action per row (only rendered when `file.id !== null` — untracked S3 objects with no DB row have no tags/content to edit) opening the same tag-chip editor + "searchable content" `Textarea` dialog Documents had, wired to the existing `updateStorageFileMeta` service call. `src/routes/_protected/documents.tsx` was deleted; `storageService.ts`'s now-unused `listStorageFiles` export (Documents' own listing call, superseded by Storage's existing `listAllStorageObjects`) was removed too. Removed the "Documents" entries from `src/components/layout/index.ts`'s nav and the Dashboard's `quickLinks` array (`index.tsx`) — both pointed at the now-deleted `/documents` route.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The merged page's Type filter only distinguishes images vs. non-images (matching Documents' original "everything that isn't an image" definition) — it doesn't further split "documents" into finer categories (PDFs vs. spreadsheets vs. text, etc.). Tag/content editing still only applies to files with a `storage_files` row (`tracked: true`/`id !== null`) — an untracked object found directly in S3 (e.g. a backup) still can't be tagged, same limitation Documents itself never had a workaround for either (it only ever listed tracked files). Weather's removal is permanent — there is no other page displaying external weather data.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all four touched/deleted files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files; `bunx biome check` on the touched files shows only two pre-existing warnings/errors that were already present in the original `storage.tsx`/`index.tsx` before this change (a static-element-interaction lint on the drag-and-drop zone, and a `useExhaustiveDependencies` note on the Dashboard's Knowledge Base stats effect) — neither is a new regression. Confirmed the generated route tree no longer lists `/weather` or `/documents`. Added a Changelog entry (v2.36.0) and a To-Do item documenting both changes, marked done.

### Fix: File preview "blocked by browser" popup and false "No preview available" errors

1. **Goal / behavior**: Two reported issues on `/storage` and `/documents` (Document Archive), both shared through `FilePreviewDialog.tsx`. (a) The row-level Download/Open action was getting silently blocked as a popup in some browsers. (b) Some real, previewable files (PDFs/images uploaded with no reported browser MIME type) showed "No preview available for this file type" instead of actually previewing.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. (a) `storage.tsx`/`documents.tsx`'s `handleDownload` now calls `window.open("", "_blank")` synchronously inside the click handler (getting a real tab handle while still inside the user gesture), *then* awaits `getStorageFileUrl` and sets `tab.location.href` — the previous code called `window.open(url, ...)` only after the `await`, which is outside the click's original user-gesture window in some browsers (notably Safari) and got silently blocked. (b) Root cause of the false negative: `file.type` from the browser's `File` object can be an empty string for some sources (e.g. certain drag-and-drop flows), and `uploadStorageFile` was storing that empty string directly as `contentType` — `FilePreviewDialog.tsx`'s `isImage`/`isPdf`/etc. checks then had nothing usable to match, so a real PDF fell through to the "no preview" fallback. `storageService.ts`'s `uploadStorageFile` now falls back to a newly-exported `guessContentType(fileName)` (extension → MIME map) when `file.type` is blank; `FilePreviewDialog.tsx` also applies the same extension-based fallback client-side (via a local `effectiveContentType()` helper, treating blank/`application/octet-stream`/`binary/octet-stream` as "unknown") so files already stored before this fix benefit too, without a data migration.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This does not add inline preview support for formats that were never supported (Office docs, archives, etc.) — those still correctly fall through to the "no inline preview" message, which now explicitly points at the Open/Download button instead of just stating the limitation.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all four touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all four touched files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.35.0) and a To-Do item documenting both fixes, marked done.

### Fix: Extract stage could show "Done" with empty output, breaking every later stage

1. **Goal / behavior**: Root-caused a recurrence of `Pipeline stopped: The "Extract text" stage must complete successfully before this stage can run.` on a **brand-new upload** (not a retry — the earlier Retry-button guard entry above was a real but separate defensive fix, not the actual trigger here). The true cause: if the AI extraction call for the `extract` stage returned an empty/whitespace-only string, the stage was still saved with `status: "done"` (just with a blank `output`) — every dependent stage (`summarize`/`tags`/`classify`) then correctly found no real extract output and failed with that message, even though Extract's own badge showed "Done", which is what confused the report ("Extract gave a Done status but the other stages failed with the same error").
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `documentPipelineService.ts`'s `extract` branch now checks `outputText.trim()` after the AI call and throws a clear `"Extraction produced no usable text from this file..."` error (failing the stage properly) instead of letting an empty result be saved as `done`. `findStageOutput()` was also hardened to treat a blank/whitespace-only stored `output` the same as missing, so any legacy row saved before this fix can't be silently read as valid input either. `document-pipeline.tsx`'s stage-detail Retry logic (`extractDone`) now also requires the extract stage to have real (trimmed non-empty) output, not just `status === "done"`, and a job whose Extract is stuck in that pre-fix "done but empty" state gets its own Retry button + explanatory hint instead of being a dead end.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This surfaces the failure earlier and more clearly (on Extract itself) — it doesn't make extraction succeed on documents that are genuinely blank/image-only/too short; those still need a different (readable) file.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in either touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on both touched files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.34.2) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Reliability improvements: Document Pipeline Resume, Workflows/Models re-sync visibility, Backups Replace mode

1. **Goal / behavior**: Implemented the three "Improve reliability / correctness" To-Do sub-issues, each scoped honestly against this template's real constraints (no server-side background job runner, no live workflow/model-list API — both documented limitations from earlier entries above). (a) **Document Pipeline** — a job left mid-run (e.g. tab closed) can now be continued with one **Resume** button (job list row + detail dialog header) instead of retrying each stage one at a time. (b) **Workflows/Models registries** — re-synced both snapshots against the live platform right now (added a newly-live workflow wasn't missing, but one new live Model, id `1211` "AI Squared", was added to `seed-models.ts`/reseeded), and both `/workflows` and `/query`'s Models tab now show "List/last synced X ago" so staleness is visible instead of silent; re-running either seed script now also **updates** existing rows' fields (previously `onConflictDoNothing`, which silently kept stale data on a field change) in addition to adding new ones. (c) **Backups** — restore now offers a real **"Replace all data"** mode alongside the existing merge/additive one, gated behind a strongly-worded confirmation.
2. **Data flow**: (a) No schema change. `document-pipeline.tsx` — new `stageNeedsRun(stage)` (a stage needs (re)running if `status !== "done"`, or if it's `extract` marked "done" but with empty output — the same edge case from the earlier "done but empty" fix entry) and `handleResumeJob(jobId, stages)`, which filters a job's current stage rows down to just the ones still needing to run and hands that subset straight to the existing `runStagesSequentially` (already generic over "which stages" and already splits extract-first/independent-parallel/report-last from any given list — no new orchestration logic needed). A "Resume" button (job row + detail dialog title) appears whenever `!isRunning && job.status !== "done" && stages.some(stageNeedsRun)`. (b) New `registry_sync_log` table (`registryName` unique, `lastSyncedAt`, `entryCount`) via migration `0018_mute_ultimatum.sql`; new `src/services/db/registrySyncService.ts` (`listRegistrySyncStatus`, read-only). `seed-workflows.ts`/`seed-models.ts` both switched their `onConflictDoNothing` to `onConflictDoUpdate` (refreshing name/description/status/etc. on re-run) and now upsert a `registry_sync_log` row (by `registryName`) with `new Date()`/the row count every time they run. `workflows.tsx` and `query.tsx` each added a `useQuery(["registrySyncStatus"], listRegistrySyncStatus)` and render the matching entry's `lastSyncedAt` via `date-fns`'s `formatDistanceToNow`. (c) `backupService.ts`'s `restoreBackup` input changed from a bare `id: number` to `{ id: number; mode?: "merge" | "replace" }` (default `"merge"`, preserving the old behavior exactly when unspecified); when `mode === "replace"` it `db.delete(table)`s every table in `BACKUP_TABLE_NAMES` *before* the existing insert loop — safe in any order since every FK among those tables uses `onDelete: "set null"`/`"cascade"` (verified against `schema.ts`, no `"no action"` FKs in the backed-up set). `BackupsPanel.tsx`'s restore `AlertDialog` gained a `RadioGroup` (Merge / Replace all data, defaulting to Merge) with distinct copy and a `variant="destructive"` confirm button when Replace is selected.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Verified the live workflow/model lists directly via the `aisquared` MCP tools (`aisquared_workflows_list`: 18 workflows, all ids/statuses already matched the existing `seed-workflows.ts` snapshot exactly — no changes needed there; `aisquared_models_list`: 18 models, exactly one — id `1211` "AI Squared", a `table_selector` model on the AI Squared Vector Store connector — missing from the existing 17-entry `seed-models.ts`, now added) before touching any seed data, per the project's live-verification convention.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: None of these add true server-side automation — Resume still requires the user (or agent) to be present to click it, and the Workflows/Models "sync" is still a manual re-run of a seed script (this app has no runtime API to list either one itself, only the `aisquared` MCP tools can, so a fully automatic background sync isn't possible here) — the new "last synced" timestamp makes that limitation visible rather than removing it. Backups' Replace mode still generates fresh identity ids on insert (same as merge mode) rather than preserving the snapshot's original ids, and still doesn't remap cross-table foreign keys to the new ids (same documented limitation as the original Backups entry) — it solves "duplicates on top of current data," not "byte-for-byte point-in-time revert."
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `registry_sync_log` in the table list). Re-ran both seed scripts live and confirmed via a direct query that `registry_sync_log` has both `workflows`/`models` rows with real timestamps and correct entry counts (18/18). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new error from a `sql` import name colliding with the seed scripts' own `neon()` client variable, aliased to `sqlTag`). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on all touched/created files — no new lint issues. Added a Changelog entry (v2.37.0) and marked the "Improve reliability / correctness" parent To-Do plus its 3 sub-issues done.

### To-Do: added a Cancel status

1. **Goal / behavior**: Tasks on `/todo` can now be **cancelled** as a distinct outcome from being completed. Each open (pending/in-progress) task card gets a new Cancel button (next to Delete); a cancelled task shows a "Reopen" button instead (sets it back to Pending) plus a Delete button. Cancelled tasks are grouped into their own collapsible "Cancelled" section below Done (collapsed by default, unlike Done which defaults open), and a new "Cancelled" filter/count joins the existing Pending/In Progress/Done tabs.
2. **Data flow**: No migration needed — `todos.status` is a plain `text()` column with a TypeScript `$type<...>()` union (not a Postgres enum), so widening it in `schema.ts` to `"pending" | "in_progress" | "done" | "cancelled"` and in `todoService.ts` (`TodoInput.status`, `updateTodoStatus`'s input type) is a type-level change only — the existing `updateTodoStatus` handler already clears `completedAt` for any non-`"done"` status, so cancelling correctly leaves/clears `completedAt` with no handler change. `todo.tsx` — new `handleSetStatus(todo, status)` (shared by new `handleCancel`/`handleReopen`, calling the same `statusMutation`/local-storage update path `handleCycleStatus` already used); `NEXT_STATUS` gained `cancelled: "pending"` so the status-icon click still only cycles pending→in_progress→done→pending (cancelling is a deliberate separate button, not something you land on while cycling) but clicking a cancelled task's icon reopens it. `isOverdue` now also excludes `"cancelled"` tasks (same treatment as `"done"`). The existing Open/Done split memo gained a third `cancelledTodos` group (`status === "cancelled"`, excluded from `openTodos`) rendered as its own `Collapsible` section.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. The local-storage fallback mode (no DB) supports cancel/reopen too, via the same `handleSetStatus` local-storage branch.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: There's no separate "cancelled at" timestamp (unlike `completedAt` for Done) — only the status itself changes; if that's wanted later it would need a new nullable column, same pattern as `completedAt`. A cancelled sub-issue still shows nested under its parent (same indentation/badge as before) inside the Cancelled section, same as Done tasks already did.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all three touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on all three touched files — no new lint issues (the one pre-existing `noExplicitAny` warning on this page is unchanged, unrelated to this change). Added a Changelog entry (v2.38.0) and a To-Do item documenting the feature, marked done.

### Fix: Anthropic requests failing with 404 (retired model ID)

1. **Goal / behavior**: Anthropic-powered features (AI Summariser, Data Explorer, Insights, Knowledge Base, AI Chat) work again — they were failing every single call with a 404.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service-shape change. `aiModelService.ts`'s `ANTHROPIC_MODEL` constant changed from `claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022` to `claude-sonnet-4-6`.
3. **Inputs/config**: By the time this was diagnosed, `ANTHROPIC_KEY` had actually been added to `.env.local` (it was still unset in the two entries above) — so the request was correctly authenticated but Anthropic rejected the *model name itself*, not the key.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Anthropic model IDs are pinned snapshots that get retired on a schedule (confirmed via Anthropic's own model-deprecations page: `claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022` was retired 2025-10-28) — `claude-sonnet-4-6` will itself eventually be retired too (tentatively "not sooner than February 17, 2027" per that page as of this writing) and will need updating again at that point; there's no auto-updating alias for production use (Anthropic deliberately doesn't offer a `-latest` pointer), so this is a one-line constant that needs occasional manual bumping.
5. **Validation**: Root-caused directly from the real Error Log entry (`Anthropic API request failed with status 404: {"type":"error","error":{"type":"not_found_error","message":"model: claude-3-5-sonnet-20241022"}}`), cross-checked against Anthropic's official model-deprecations documentation, then made a real live API call with the app's actual `ANTHROPIC_KEY` secret using the new model ID — got a real 200 response with valid message content. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Marked the corresponding Error Log entry resolved. Added a Changelog entry (v2.25.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### AI Insights: data-only connector picker, schema discovery for every data source, cached fallback

1. **Goal / behavior**: Three changes to `/insights`'s Data Source picker. (a) It now only lists true data-source connectors — AI/ML model connectors (e.g. Anthropic/OpenAI/AI Squared Bolt) no longer appear, since they're the model used to generate insight text, not data to analyze. (b) Live SQL schema discovery is attempted for every SQL-capable engine, not just Postgresql/MariaDB/Oracle. (c) When live discovery isn't possible for a connector at all (not a SQL engine) or fails (a broken live connection), the page falls back to a cached schema snapshot instead of just an error, clearly labeled as cached.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/DB change. `insights.tsx` — the connector list query changed from `useConnectors({ type: "source" })` to `useConnectors({ type: "source", category: "data" })` (verified live via `aisquared_connectors_list` that `category: "data"` excludes AI/ML connectors, matching the same server-side filter already used by `/connectors`' category dropdown). `ConnectorDetailSheet.tsx`'s shared `DISCOVERY_QUERIES` map (already exported/reused, per the earlier "Expanded Connectors schema discovery to Oracle" entry above) gained a live-verified `Bigquery` catalog query (region-qualified `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS`, confirmed working against the real connector) plus best-effort queries for `Snowflake`/`Databricks`/`Clickhouse`/`AwsAthena` (the query text is correct for each engine, but their live test connectors in this workspace currently error for unrelated environment reasons — missing ODBC driver, a platform request error, a `system.columns` failure, and a pagination incompatibility respectively — all re-verified live via `aisquared_connectors_query_source` before writing this). New `src/lib/connectorSchemaFallback.ts` — a `CONNECTOR_SCHEMA_FALLBACK` map (keyed by this workspace's real connector ids) holding real table/stream names + field lists captured via `aisquared_connectors_discover` for connectors that aren't SQL engines at all (QuickBooks, SFTP, OneDrive x2, Salesforce, Odoo, Qdrant, Pinecone) or whose live connection currently errors (Databricks, Athena). `insights.tsx`'s schema-discovery `useEffect` now: skips straight to the fallback map when the provider has no `DISCOVERY_QUERIES` entry at all; falls back to it on a live query error; and only shows the old plain "couldn't discover schema" alert when neither a live query nor a fallback snapshot exists. New `isFallbackSchema`/`fallbackNote` state drives an inline `Alert` over the table picker when showing cached data, and both `handleGenerate`/`handleAsk` now reject with a clear toast instead of attempting a query when the current schema came from the fallback (querying a non-SQL connector via `querySource`, which is SQL-only, would just fail).
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. `connectorSchemaFallback.ts`'s snapshots are keyed by this specific workspace's connector ids — they're a point-in-time cache (captured via the `aisquared` MCP `connectors_discover` tool, the same underlying capability the Connectors page's own schema browser uses), not a live query; a connector id not in the map (e.g. a newly added one, or a different workspace) simply reports no schema available, same as before this change.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Live SQL discovery genuinely works today for Postgresql/MariaDB/Oracle/BigQuery (all 4 re-verified with real rows via `aisquared_connectors_query_source`); Snowflake/Databricks/ClickHouse/Athena's *queries* are correct but their specific live connectors in this workspace error for connector/environment reasons outside the app's control, so those 4 currently fall back to their cached snapshot instead. The cached fallback is inherently static — it won't reflect schema changes made in the source system after the snapshot was taken, and there is no way for the running app to refresh it itself (the platform's discover/catalog capability is only exposed as an `aisquared` MCP tool, not through the app's own tRPC layer, same limitation documented for schema discovery in earlier entries above) — refreshing it requires re-running `connectors_discover` and updating the file, same as a manual seed-script re-run elsewhere in this app. Because fallback-sourced tables can't be queried, "Generate Insights"/"Ask a Question" only ever work against the 4 genuinely-live SQL engines.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified every claim before writing code: `aisquared_connectors_list` (category `data` correctly excludes AI/ML connectors), `aisquared_connectors_query_source` against real Postgresql/MariaDB/Oracle/BigQuery/Snowflake/ClickHouse/Databricks/Athena/Salesforce/Odoo connectors (confirming which actually work vs. which error and why), and `aisquared_connectors_discover` against every non-SQL connector in the workspace (QuickBooks/SFTP/OneDrive x2/Salesforce/Odoo/Qdrant/Pinecone/Databricks/Athena) to capture the real fallback snapshot data. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all three touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all three files; `bunx biome check` shows the same pre-existing `useExhaustiveDependencies` pattern already present elsewhere in this file/its sibling (deliberate, suppressed via `eslint-disable-next-line` per the existing convention) — not a new regression. Confirmed `/insights` and `/connectors` still return 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.42.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: AI Insights unnecessarily blocked Generate Insights/Ask a Question for cached-schema connectors

1. **Goal / behavior**: Generate Insights and Ask a Question now work for connectors whose schema came from the cached fallback (OneDrive, QuickBooks, Odoo, and similar), not just the 4 genuinely-live SQL engines documented in the entry above.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `insights.tsx` — removed the `isFallbackSchema` guard (and its `disabled` wiring on both buttons) from `handleGenerate`/`handleAsk`; both now always attempt `loadSelectedTableData()` (already a plain `SELECT * FROM <qualifiedName> LIMIT 200` per table) regardless of how the table list was populated. The cached-snapshot `Alert` shown above the table picker was reworded to describe only the table/column *list* as cached, not data querying.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This corrects a wrong assumption made in the previous entry, not a platform change — `querySource`'s `SELECT * FROM <table>` form was always able to run against these connectors' native APIs (each stream/model name simply maps to the connector's own object), it just isn't full ANSI SQL (no `WHERE`/`JOIN`/aggregate support verified), which is why schema *discovery* still needs the cached fallback for non-SQL engines even though row *fetching* doesn't. A connector that's broken at the live-connection level regardless of query shape (e.g. Salesforce and SFTP in this workspace, confirmed via direct live testing) still fails at query time — same as any other connector error, surfaced through the existing error handling, not a regression from this fix.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified via `aisquared_connectors_query_source` before writing any code: `SELECT * FROM "Booklet.xlsx"` (OneDrive), `SELECT * FROM Customer` (QuickBooks), and `SELECT * FROM res.partner` (Odoo) all returned real rows despite none of these being SQL engines; `SFTP`/`Salesforce` still genuinely fail at the connection level (unrelated to query syntax), confirming the fix doesn't paper over real connector problems. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Confirmed `/insights` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.43.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: Insights generation failing with AIS API 401

1. **Goal / behavior**: "Generate Insights" on `/insights` (reported error: `Insights generation failed: AIS API request failed with status 401`) works again.
2. **Data flow**: Root cause: `runModel` called the platform's AI/ML connector (id `1803`, "AI Squared Bolt 2") through `useExecuteModel()` -> tRPC `connectors.executeModel` -> `apiFetch` (`/base/src/server/platform/request.ts`), which was returning a 401 from the *platform's own auth layer* on this app's outbound request — live-verified via the `aisquared` MCP `connectors_execute_model` tool that connector 1803 itself works fine and returns real completions, isolating the failure to this app's own request/session authentication against the platform API, not the connector or model being broken. `insights.tsx` — `runModel` now calls `aiModel.mutate` (`useAiModel`, the same direct OpenAI/Anthropic secret-backed hook "Ask a Question" already used, added in the earlier "AI features moved off the failing AI/ML connector" entry above) instead of `executeModel.mutate`/`MODEL_CONNECTOR_ID`, parsing the response with the shared `extractProviderText` instead of the now-removed local `extractModelText`. A new `generateProvider` state + `AiProviderSelect` next to the Generate Insights button lets the user pick Anthropic/OpenAI for this action too (previously it wasn't user-selectable at all, being hardcoded to the one platform connector). `useExecuteModel`/`MODEL_CONNECTOR_ID`/`extractModelText` were all removed as dead code — nothing else in this file used them.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config — reuses the existing `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_KEY` secrets already wired up for "Ask a Question" and every other AI feature migrated in the earlier entry.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: AI Chat's "AI Squared Bolt 2" model option still goes through the same platform connector (id `1803`) and was explicitly out of scope for this fix (the user only reported the Insights failure) — it carries the same latent 401 risk if the platform's auth layer has the same issue there, since it's the identical `executeModel`/`apiFetch` path; not touched here per the project's "match scope precisely" rule.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified connector `1803` works fine at the platform level via `aisquared_connectors_execute_model` (real chat-completion response) before concluding the 401 was app-side, not the connector. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file; `bunx biome check` shows the same pre-existing `useExhaustiveDependencies` pattern already present in this file (deliberate, unrelated to this change). Confirmed `/insights` still returns 200 from the dev server. Marked the corresponding Error Log entry resolved. Added a Changelog entry (v2.45.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: Error Log details were cut off

1. **Goal / behavior**: The Error Log table's message column was truncated to one line (only visible in full via a hover tooltip), and the full stack trace wasn't viewable anywhere. Clicking anywhere on an error's row now opens a details dialog showing the complete message and full stack trace, plus Resolve/Delete.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `error-log.tsx` — new `selectedError: ErrorLog | null` state; each `TableRow` gained `onClick={() => setSelectedError(err)}` and a `cursor-pointer` class; the row's own Resolve/Delete buttons call `e.stopPropagation()` inside their `onClick` handlers so clicking them doesn't also open the dialog. A new `Dialog` (open when `selectedError !== null`) renders the error's id/severity/status/source/timestamp in the header, the full message in a wrapped (`whitespace-pre-wrap break-words`) block, and the stack trace (if present) in a scrollable monospace `<pre>` block, with Resolve/Delete actions in the footer that also close the dialog after acting.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: None new — this is a pure detail-view addition on top of the existing table/filter/pagination behavior, which is unchanged.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome check`/`format --write` on the touched file — fixed two real `a11y` lint errors from an initial wrapper-`div` `stopPropagation()` approach by moving `stopPropagation()` into each button's own `onClick` instead. Confirmed `/error-log` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.44.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### AI Squared Bolt selectable everywhere; AI Insights unstructured-connector handling; BigQuery quoting fix

1. **Goal / behavior**: Three follow-ups. (a) Every AI-provider picker in the app (`AiProviderSelect`) now offers a third option, **AI Squared Bolt**, alongside Anthropic/OpenAI. (b) AI Insights detects connectors flagged as unstructured document sources (`configuration.data_type === "unstructured"`, e.g. OneDrive's Unstructured mode) and reduces their row-fetch limit (20 vs 200) plus truncates very long text fields before building the AI prompt. (c) Fixed BigQuery table data failing to load on AI Insights.
2. **Data flow**: (a) `aiProviders.ts`'s `AI_PROVIDERS` gained `{id: "ai-squared-bolt", label: "AI Squared Bolt"}` plus `AI_SQUARED_BOLT_CONNECTOR_ID = 1803` (confirmed live via `aisquared_connectors_show`/`connectors_execute_model` that this connector works fine at the platform level). `useAiModel()` was rewritten from a single `useMutation` into a small dispatcher: `anthropic`/`openai` still go through `callAiModel` (direct-secret path); `ai-squared-bolt` now goes through `useExecuteModel()` (the tRPC `connectors.executeModel` procedure, cookie/session-authenticated) — both branches return the same `{ data: unknown[] }` shape so every existing `extractProviderText(res?.data)` call site needed zero changes. Since Document Pipeline runs entirely server-side (no client-driven `useExecuteModel` call to piggyback on), `aiModelService.ts` gained a standalone `callAiSquaredBoltConnector()` that reuses the exact same `apiFetch`/`buildLlmPayload` logic the tRPC procedure uses, but built from a plain `createServerFn` handler using `getCookie(APP_AUTH_COOKIE_NAME)` directly (verified `apiFetch` only reads `ctx.authToken`, not `ctx.request`/`ctx.responseHeaders`, so a minimal hand-built context is sufficient) — `callAiText()` now branches to it for `"ai-squared-bolt"`. `documentPipelineService.ts`'s `AiProvider` union and `schema.ts`'s two `provider` columns' `$type<>()` were widened to include `"ai-squared-bolt"` (a `text()` column type-level change, no migration needed — matches the project's no-`pgEnum` rule). (b) `insights.tsx` — `isUnstructuredConnector` reads `selectedConnector?.attributes.configuration?.data_type === "unstructured"` directly off the connector's own config (no new endpoint); `TABLE_ROW_LIMIT` (20 vs 200) and `truncateRowForPrompt()` (per-field 4,000-char cap with a `"...[truncated, N chars total]"` marker) apply in `loadSelectedTableData()`, shared by both Generate Insights and Ask a Question. Then, per a *follow-up* request, unstructured connectors were removed from the picker entirely — `sourceConnectors` now filters out any connector with `configuration.data_type === "unstructured"` before the list even reaches the `Select`, so `isUnstructuredConnector` (and its row-limit/truncation logic, and the "Unstructured (documents)" badge) is currently unreachable dead-but-harmless code, kept in place in case that restriction is lifted later. (c) `quoteIdent(name, connectorProvider)` gained a second parameter: it now wraps identifiers in backticks for `Bigquery` specifically and ANSI double quotes for every other engine — live-verified that `"dataset"."table"` throws a BigQuery syntax error while `` `dataset`.`table` `` returns real rows; both call sites (the fallback-schema builder and the live-discovery success handler) now pass `connectorProvider` through.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config for (a)/(c). (b)'s connector-exclusion filter has no config either — it's a hardcoded `!== "unstructured"` check against the platform's own `configuration.data_type` field.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: (a) AI Chat's existing "AI Squared Bolt 2" option (hardcoded connector id 1803, from an earlier entry) is now redundant with the new generic `"ai-squared-bolt"` provider option, but wasn't consolidated — out of scope for this change. (b) Unstructured connectors are fully hidden from the picker "for now" per an explicit follow-up request — the row-limit/truncation code for them still exists but currently never runs; re-enabling would just mean removing the new filter, not rebuilding the handling. (c) Only `Bigquery` was special-cased for quoting — no other engine in `DISCOVERY_QUERIES` was found to need non-ANSI quoting during live testing.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified `AI_SQUARED_BOLT_CONNECTOR_ID` (1803) works via `aisquared_connectors_execute_model` before wiring it in. Live-verified the BigQuery quoting bug and fix directly: `SELECT * FROM "Test_Dataset"."Small_Data"` → syntax error; `` SELECT * FROM `Test_Dataset`.`Small_Data` `` → real row, via `aisquared_connectors_query_source`. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all six touched files (`aiProviders.ts`, `useAiModel.ts`, `aiModelService.ts`, `documentPipelineService.ts`, `schema.ts`, `insights.tsx`; same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files; `bunx biome check` showed only the same pre-existing `useExhaustiveDependencies` pattern (deliberate, documented in earlier entries) plus a pre-existing `useOptionalChain` info-level suggestion in `documentPipelineService.ts`, neither a new regression. Confirmed `/insights` and `/document-pipeline` still return 200 from the dev server. Added three Changelog entries (v2.46.0, v2.47.0, v2.48.0) and three To-Do items, all marked done.

### Fix: AI Insights 422 on fallback-schema/unstructured connectors from an added LIMIT clause
