1. **Goal / behavior**: Loading table rows on AI Insights for non-SQL fallback-schema connectors (Salesforce, Odoo, QuickBooks, OneDrive, etc.) no longer fails with `AIS API request failed with status 422`.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. Root cause: the unstructured-connector row-limit work above added a SQL `LIMIT` clause to every `loadSelectedTableData()` query, including fallback-schema (non-SQL) connectors, whose query engines translate `SELECT * FROM <stream>` into a native API call and correctly reject an appended `LIMIT` as invalid syntax — confirmed live via `aisquared_connectors_query_source` (`SELECT * FROM Customer LIMIT 5` against QuickBooks fails; the same query with no `LIMIT` succeeds). `insights.tsx` — `loadSelectedTableData()` now builds the query without `LIMIT` when `isFallbackSchema` is true, and instead slices the returned array to `TABLE_ROW_LIMIT` client-side after fetching (applied uniformly, so real SQL engines — which still receive `LIMIT` in the query itself — are unaffected).
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Fallback-schema connectors with no row cap enforced server-side will still transfer their full result set over the wire before the client-side slice trims it for the prompt — acceptable given these are already the smaller/slower-to-query non-SQL sources and the existing row caps (200, or 20 for unstructured connectors, which are now excluded from the picker entirely per the entry below) are modest.
5. **Validation**: Live-reproduced the exact 422 via `aisquared_connectors_query_source` with a `LIMIT` clause against QuickBooks/OneDrive/Odoo, then confirmed the identical query without `LIMIT` succeeds. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Confirmed `/insights` still returns 200 from the dev server. Resolved the 3 related Error Log entries. Added a Changelog entry (v2.49.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### AI Insights: removed Unstructured connectors from the Data Source picker (for now)

1. **Goal / behavior**: Connectors flagged as unstructured document sources (`configuration.data_type === "unstructured"`, e.g. OneDrive's Unstructured mode) no longer appear at all in the Data Source picker on `/insights` — a follow-up narrowing of the unstructured-connector handling added in the entry above, per an explicit "for now, remove access" request.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `insights.tsx` — the `sourceConnectors` memo now filters `connectorsRes?.data` down to `c.attributes.configuration?.data_type !== "unstructured"` before it ever reaches the `Select`/default-selection logic, so an unstructured connector can no longer be chosen at all (previously it was selectable, just row-limited/truncated and clearly labeled). The unstructured-specific UI (badge, description hint) and prompt-truncation logic added in the entry above are unreachable while this filter is active but were left in place, not deleted, so the restriction can be lifted later by removing just this filter.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This is an explicit, temporary restriction, not a technical limitation — the underlying unstructured-connector support (reduced row limit, text truncation) still exists in the code and still works if the filter is removed. Structured/tabular connectors are completely unaffected.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Confirmed `/insights` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.50.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Theme Generator: dark/light auto-variant, component-level accents, export/import via Storage, lock indicator, automatic backups, consistency checker, themed empty/loading states, motion consistency

1. **Goal / behavior**: Eight enhancements to the existing `/theme` builder and its app-wide `ThemeApplier`. (a) A theme can now define a **dark-mode variant** (separate background color + overlay color) that auto-swaps in for visitors whose device is in dark mode, via `prefers-color-scheme`. (b) **Component-level accents** — a "Corner Radius" and "Shadow Intensity" slider per theme, plus the theme's first accent color now also drives `--ring`/`--sidebar-primary`/`--sidebar-ring` (previously only `--accent`). (c) Each saved theme can be **exported** to a downloadable JSON file (uploaded to Storage's `exports/` folder) and a previously exported file can be **imported** back as a new theme (via `uploads/`). (d) A small fixed pill near the top-right of every page — the **theme lock indicator** — shows whether the current page has its own theme, is inheriting the global one, or has none, linking to `/theme`. (e) Deleting a theme now writes an **automatic backup snapshot** first; a new "Recently Removed" section lists recent backups with a one-click Restore (creates a new theme, never overwrites). (f) A **consistency checker** in the builder's Advanced tab shows a plain-language warning when the chosen overlay color/opacity would make text hard to read (WCAG contrast ratio). (g) Empty/loading states (`Skeleton`, `Empty`'s icon) now pick up a faint tint of the active theme's accent color instead of staying neutral. (h) Cards/dialogs/popovers get a very subtle echo of the active theme's background animation (if any) instead of the motion being confined to the backdrop layer.
2. **Data flow**: `custom_themes` gained `radius`, `shadow_strength` (both nullable ints), `dark_background`, `dark_background_key`, `dark_overlay_color`, `dark_accent_colors` (all nullable — a theme without them behaves exactly as before, single-mode) via migration `0021_same_venom.sql`. New `theme_backups` table (`original_theme_id`, `name`, `snapshot` JSON text, `reason: "overwrite"|"delete"`) — `themeService.ts`'s `deleteCustomTheme` now snapshots the row into it before deleting; new `listThemeBackups`/`restoreThemeBackup` (the latter inserts a brand-new theme from the snapshot, suffixed "(restored)"). New `src/services/db/themeExportService.ts`: `exportTheme(themeId)` uploads the theme's JSON to `exports/themes/theme-<id>-<slug>.json` and returns a presigned URL; `importThemeFromUpload(formData)` uploads the picked file to `uploads/theme-imports/`, downloads+parses it back, validates the shape, deletes the transient upload, and inserts a new theme (suffixed "(imported)"). `src/lib/customTheme.ts` gained `resolveThemeForColorScheme(theme, isDark)` (returns the effective background/backgroundKey/overlayColor/accentColors for the current color scheme) and `checkThemeConsistency()`/`contrastRatio()` (WCAG relative-luminance-based). `ThemeApplier.tsx` now tracks `window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)")`, resolves through `resolveThemeForColorScheme`, and additionally sets `--ring`/`--sidebar-primary`/`--sidebar-ring`/`--radius`/`--theme-shadow-opacity`/`--theme-anim-name` on `<html>` (previously only `--accent`+`--theme-accent-1/2/3`). New `src/components/theme/ThemeLockIndicator.tsx` (mounted in `_protected.tsx` next to `ThemeApplier`) reads the same `listActiveThemes`/`listCustomThemes` queries to render the pill. `styles.css` — box-shadow on themed surfaces now reads `--theme-shadow-opacity` (falls back to the previous fixed 35%), a new animation rule applies `--theme-anim-name` (subtler duration) to cards/dialogs/popovers, and `[data-slot="skeleton"]`/`[data-slot="empty-icon"]` pick up `--theme-accent-1`.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses the already-provisioned `DATABASE_URL` and S3 storage (both already active for the pre-existing theme gallery/persistence). Export/import intentionally goes through Storage (not a copy-pasteable text code) per an explicit user request.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The dark variant only overrides background/overlay/accent colors, not `radius`/`shadowStrength`/noise/animation — a theme's non-color "component-level" settings stay the same in both light and dark. Import only accepts the exact JSON shape this app's own Export produces (validated by checking for `name`/`backgroundType`/`background`); a hand-edited or foreign file that's missing those fields is rejected with a toast rather than partially imported. Backups are DB-only (not also mirrored to Storage) and only triggered on delete, not on every field-level edit/overwrite (there's no "edit in place" flow today — themes are only created/duplicated/deleted, so "overwrite" backups are reserved for future use if an edit-in-place flow is added). The consistency checker is a heuristic (contrast ratio against the fixed near-white theme text color + a low-overlay-opacity check) shown only in the builder, not enforced — a user can still save/activate a theme that fails it.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `theme_backups` in the table list, `custom_themes` column count increased). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new unused-import error). `bunx biome format --write` on every touched/created file; `bunx biome check` showed only the same pre-existing `noArrayIndexKey` patterns already present in this file before this change (accent-color chip lists) — not a new regression. Confirmed `routeTree.gen.ts` still lists `/theme` and the app's DB status still reports `connected`. Added a Changelog entry (v2.55.0) and a To-Do item documenting all eight changes, marked done.

### Fix/change: Theme dark variant and backups switched from automatic to manual

1. **Goal / behavior**: Per explicit follow-up request, two previously-automatic theme behaviors are now manual. (a) The dark-mode variant no longer follows the visitor's device/browser `prefers-color-scheme` setting — a Light/Dark toggle button now appears next to the theme lock indicator (top-right of every page) whenever the currently active theme has a dark variant defined, and the visitor switches it themselves; it never flips on its own. (b) Theme backups are no longer snapshotted automatically before a delete — a new "Back up theme now" button on each saved theme card lets the user snapshot it on demand instead. The "Recently Removed" section was renamed "Your Backups" to match.
2. **Data flow**: No migration needed. `custom_themes`/`theme_backups` schema unchanged except `theme_backups.reason`'s `$type` union gained `"manual"` (a text column, type-level only). `src/lib/customTheme.ts` — added `getManualColorScheme()`/`setManualColorScheme()` (a `localStorage` key `theme-manual-color-scheme`, firing a `theme-color-scheme-changed` window `CustomEvent` on write for same-tab listeners). `ThemeApplier.tsx` — removed the `window.matchMedia("(prefers-color-scheme: dark)")` listener entirely; now reads the manual preference on mount and listens for the custom event + cross-tab `storage` event instead. `ThemeLockIndicator.tsx` — resolves the current page's active theme (same `resolveActiveTheme` used elsewhere) to check `darkBackground` is set; when it is, renders a Sun/Moon icon button next to the pill that calls `setManualColorScheme()`. `themeService.ts` — `deleteCustomTheme` no longer inserts into `theme_backups`; new `backupCustomTheme(id)` server function does that snapshot on explicit request instead. `theme.tsx` — added a `backupMutation`/`handleBackupNow` and a "Back up theme now" button (History icon) next to Export on each saved theme card; the delete confirmation dialog's copy no longer implies an automatic backup; the Dark Mode Variant toggle's helper text now describes the manual switch instead of automatic device detection.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. The manual color-scheme preference is per-browser (`localStorage`), matching the pattern already used elsewhere in this app for local UI-only preferences.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The Light/Dark toggle only appears when the active theme actually has a dark variant configured — themes without one show no toggle (nothing to switch to). A backup is only ever created when the user clicks "Back up theme now"; deleting a theme without having backed it up first is unrecoverable, called out directly in the delete confirmation dialog's copy.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on all touched files — only the same pre-existing `noArrayIndexKey` patterns already present before this change, not a new regression. Added a Changelog entry (v2.56.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Improvement: Theme Generator's "Surprise Me" now randomizes the dark variant and gives themes a random name

1. **Goal / behavior**: The Surprise Me button on `/theme` now (a) about half the time also randomizes the Dark Mode Variant (dark background + dark overlay color), so a Surprise Me theme can exercise the manual Light/Dark toggle described in the entry above, instead of the dark variant always being off; (b) always assigns each generated theme a fun random name (e.g. "Cosmic Nebula 482") instead of the previous fixed "Surprise Theme" placeholder (which only applied when the Name field was empty).
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `theme.tsx` — new `SURPRISE_ADJECTIVES`/`SURPRISE_NOUNS` word lists and a `randomThemeName()` helper (adjective + noun + a random 3-digit suffix). `handleSurpriseMe` now: rolls a `Math.random() < 0.5` check to decide `includeDarkVariant`, calling `setDarkEnabled`/`setDarkSolidColor`/`setDarkOverlayColor` with fresh `randomHex()` values when true (leaving `darkEnabled` false otherwise, same as before); unconditionally calls `setName(randomThemeName())` (previously only set a name if the field was blank); the success toast now mentions the dark variant when one was generated.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Calling Surprise Me always overwrites the Name field now (previously it only filled in a name if empty) — a user who had already typed a custom name before clicking Surprise Me will have it replaced with a new random one, which matches the spirit of "Surprise Me" randomizing the whole theme.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on the touched file — same pre-existing `noArrayIndexKey` patterns as before, not a new regression. Added a Changelog entry (v2.57.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: exported themes now saved into an "Exported Theme" Storage folder

1. **Goal / behavior**: Exporting a theme from `/theme` now saves its JSON file into a clearly named `Exported Theme` folder in Storage (a subfolder under the app's private `exports/` prefix), instead of the previous generic `exports/themes/` path — makes exported theme files easy to find as a distinct group.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `themeExportService.ts` — added `EXPORTED_THEME_FOLDER = "exports/Exported Theme"` and pointed `exportTheme`'s `uploadFile` key at `${EXPORTED_THEME_FOLDER}/theme-<id>-<slug>.json` instead of `exports/themes/...`. `theme.tsx`'s export success toast now says `Saved to Storage → "Exported Theme" folder — download started.` instead of the generic "Theme exported to Storage" message. Import (`importThemeFromUpload`) is unaffected — it still uses the transient `uploads/theme-imports/` path and deletes the file after parsing, since imports aren't meant to persist as a browsable folder.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: `/storage`'s folder-listing logic derives a file's top-level "folder" from the first path segment before the first `/` (i.e. `exports`), so `Exported Theme` shows as a subfolder/prefix within the existing Exports grouping rather than as its own top-level folder tab — consistent with how every other `exports/<subfolder>/...` convention in this app already works (e.g. `exports/backups/`, `exports/document-pipeline/`).
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on both touched files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.58.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Improvement: darker badges/tables on Data Manager, Import Wizard, Storage, Changelog, and Audit Log

1. **Goal / behavior**: Badges ("bubbles" — status/category/tag/target pills) and data tables on 5 pages — Data Manager, Import Wizard, Storage, Changelog, Audit Log — now render with a darker look (dark backgrounds, light text) instead of this app's default light styling, for better contrast in these data-dense views. There is no global light/dark mode toggle in this app, so this is a targeted per-page styling change, not a theme-system change.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/data change — pure CSS/markup. `styles.css` — added a `.dark-surface` utility class (`@apply rounded-lg border border-border bg-background p-1`) meant to always be paired with the app's pre-existing (previously unused) `.dark` class, which already defines a full dark-palette CSS-variable block (`--background`, `--card`, `--muted`, `--secondary`, `--border`, `--foreground`, etc. — the same block Tailwind's `dark:` variant is configured against via `@custom-variant dark (&:is(.dark *))`). Because `Table`/`TableHeader`/`TableRow`/`TableCell` and `Badge` (both base-owned, read-only components) already build their styling entirely from `bg-card`/`bg-muted`/`bg-secondary`/`border-border`/`text-foreground` utilities (which read these CSS variables), wrapping a `<Table>` or a badge row in `className="dark dark-surface"` flips its whole look to the dark palette with zero edits to the shared components themselves. Applied to: `audit.tsx`'s log table; `changelog.tsx`'s per-entry row (badge + card); `storage.tsx`'s file table; `import-wizard.tsx`'s CSV preview table, import-history table, and imported-rows dialog table (plus the standalone row-count badge); `data-manager.tsx`'s Exports and Reports tables.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This only darkens the specific badge/table surfaces that were wrapped — surrounding page chrome (headers, filter controls, cards outside the wrapped areas) is unchanged and stays in the app's default light palette, so each affected page now has a deliberately darker "panel" for its data table sitting inside an otherwise light page, rather than a full-page dark mode. Dialog-internal tag-editor badges on Storage (a separate small badge cluster in the tag-editing dialog, not the main file table) were left as-is since the request was specifically about "bubbles and table" in the main page views.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all six touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on all six touched files — the one finding (`noStaticElementInteractions` on Storage's drag-and-drop upload zone) is the same pre-existing, already-documented issue from an earlier entry above, unrelated to this change. Added a Changelog entry (v2.59.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Improvement: badges/tables on those 5 pages now match the side panel's exact color theme (refines the entry above)

1. **Goal / behavior**: Follow-up refinement — the badges/tables on Data Manager, Import Wizard, Storage, Changelog, and Audit Log now use the exact same background/text/border colors as the app's side navigation panel, instead of the generic app-wide dark palette used in the previous entry. This means they track whatever the sidebar currently looks like: light gray in the default theme, or the translucent dark-glass look when a Theme Generator custom theme is active — always matching, never a separate/independent dark look.
2. **Data flow**: `styles.css` — replaced `.dark-surface` with `.sidebar-surface`, which keeps the same `rounded-lg border p-1` shape but instead of pairing with the app's generic `.dark` class, directly remaps the semantic tokens `Table`/`Badge` already consume (`--background`, `--foreground`, `--card`/`--card-foreground`, `--popover`/`--popover-foreground`, `--muted`/`--muted-foreground`, `--secondary`/`--secondary-foreground`, `--accent`/`--accent-foreground`, `--border`, `--input`) to this app's existing `--sidebar`/`--sidebar-foreground`/`--sidebar-accent`/`--sidebar-accent-foreground`/`--sidebar-border` variables. Updated all five pages' wrapper `className`s from `"dark dark-surface ..."` to `"sidebar-surface ..."` (no `.dark` class anymore); `changelog.tsx`'s per-entry row wrapper simplified from `"dark flex ... bg-card p-3 text-card-foreground"` to `"sidebar-surface flex ... p-3"`.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Same structural scope as the previous entry (only the wrapped Table/badge surfaces change, not full pages). Because this now reads live `--sidebar*` tokens, these surfaces will visually shift automatically if the sidebar's own colors ever change (e.g. a future sidebar restyle or a custom theme override) — that's intentional per this request ("same...as the side panel"), not a bug.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on all five touched files plus `styles.css` — same pre-existing `noStaticElementInteractions` note on Storage's drag-and-drop zone only, not a new regression. Added a Changelog entry (v2.60.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix/refactor: AI Chat's "AI Squared Bolt" option consolidated onto the shared provider dispatcher

1. **Goal / behavior**: User reported "AI request failed" in AI Chat when using "AI Squared Bolt". Root-caused: an initial diagnostic curl to the app's own `connectors.executeModel` tRPC procedure with no session cookie returned a 401 — but this was a false lead (that procedure requires `ctx.authToken`, which only exists for a real logged-in session; an unauthenticated curl will always 401 regardless of connector). Live-verified via the `aisquared` MCP tools that connector 1803 ("AI Squared Bolt 2") itself responds correctly. The real issue found on closer look: `chat.tsx` had its own separate/duplicated connector-calling code (`useExecuteModel()` + a local `providerForConnectorId`/`extractReply`) instead of using the shared `"ai-squared-bolt"` provider option already built into `@/lib/aiProviders`/`@/hooks/useAiModel` and used by every other AI-powered page (AI Insights, Data Explorer, Document Pipeline, etc. — see the "AI Squared Bolt selectable everywhere" entry above). Per the user's request, `chat.tsx` was refactored to use that shared path for all three model options instead of only two of them.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `chat.tsx` — removed the local `useExecuteModel()` call, the local `extractReply()` helper, and `providerForConnectorId()`'s `null` branch; `MODEL_OPTIONS`' third entry now uses `AI_SQUARED_BOLT_CONNECTOR_ID` (imported from `@/lib/aiProviders` instead of a locally hardcoded `1803`) with the label "AI Squared Bolt" (dropped the "2" to match the rest of the app); `providerForConnectorId()` now always returns a valid `AiProviderId` (`"anthropic" | "openai" | "ai-squared-bolt"`, never `null`); `handleSend` now always calls `aiModel.mutate({ connectorId: provider, messages })` (the shared `useAiModel()` dispatcher, which internally routes `"ai-squared-bolt"` through `useExecuteModel()` against the real connector and the other two through direct-secret calls) instead of branching between `aiModel`/`executeModel`; reply parsing now uses the shared `extractProviderText()` from `aiProviders.ts` instead of a duplicated local copy.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. No connector id changed — still the same `AI_SQUARED_BOLT_CONNECTOR_ID = 1803` used everywhere else in the app.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This does not change how the underlying connector authenticates — if the platform's connector-execution auth genuinely fails for a real logged-in user (not verifiable from this environment without a live browser session), the error would still surface the same way through `useAiModel()`'s `"ai-squared-bolt"` branch, since it's the same `useExecuteModel()` call underneath; this fix's scope was removing the duplicated/inconsistent code path in `chat.tsx`, not changing the connector's own auth behavior.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified connector 1803 ("AI Squared Bolt 2") returns a real completion via `aisquared_connectors_execute_model`. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.61.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: AI Chat shows a clear message when AI Squared Bolt is rejected with a 401

1. **Goal / behavior**: The predicted "latent 401 risk" called out in the entry above materialized for real: a real user session got `TRPCClientError: AIS API request failed with status 401` sending a message with the "AI Squared Bolt" model. Root-caused to the platform's own authorization layer rejecting AI/ML connector *execution* for this session specifically — the connector itself works fine (live-verified via the `aisquared` MCP tool), and the same session can successfully call other connector operations (`querySource`, used throughout `/connectors`/`/insights`/`/query`) without issue; only *executing a model* through the connector is rejected. This is the same class of failure previously root-caused for Insights (see the "Fix: Insights generation failing with AIS API 401" entry above) — a platform-side permission gate that app code cannot grant, and unlike Anthropic/OpenAI, AI Squared Bolt has no direct external API to bypass it with. Per the user's explicit choice (asked via the `question` tool), AI Chat now shows a clear, specific explanation when this happens instead of a generic failure message, pointing the user at Anthropic/OpenAI as working alternatives.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/connector change. `chat.tsx` — new `aiSquaredBoltUnavailableMessage()` helper; `onAiError` now checks `isUnauthorized(error) && provider === "ai-squared-bolt"` (checked before the existing generic `isUnauthorized` branch) and shows that specific message via a longer-duration `toast.error(..., { duration: 10000 })` instead of the generic "You are not authorized to use this model." wording used for other 401s.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This does not and cannot restore AI Squared Bolt's functionality for affected sessions — that requires the platform itself to grant AI model-execution permission to the account in question, which is entirely outside what any code change in this app can control. The user explicitly chose to keep the option visible with a clear error (over removing it or leaving the generic message) — if the underlying platform permission is never granted, this option will continue to visibly fail for affected accounts, just with an honest explanation instead of a confusing generic one.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.61.1) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: production build failure blocking Publish/Deploy (SSR code leaking into the client bundle)

1. **Goal / behavior**: `bun run build` (and therefore Publish/Deploy, which runs it) was failing with a Rollup error: `"Readable" is not exported by "__vite-browser-external"`, originating from `@tanstack/router-core`'s SSR-only `transformStreamWithRouter.js`. The app now builds successfully.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/UI change — this was a pure build/bundling bug, invisible in dev (`bun run build`/`vite build` is explicitly not run during routine development per this project's own rules, which is why it went undetected until an actual deploy attempt). Root cause, found by actually running `bun run build` and bisecting which file's code was reachable from the client bundle: `aiModelService.ts`'s `callAiSquaredBoltConnector()` (added in the "AI Squared Bolt selectable everywhere" entry above) is called by the plain, non-`createServerFn`-wrapped exported function `callAiText()` — and because `useAiModel.ts` (a client-side hook, used by `chat.tsx`/`insights.tsx`/etc.) statically imports `callAiModel` from that same file, Vite's production client build resolves and bundles *everything* in the file, not just the parts reachable through an actual `.handler()` closure (which is the only code TanStack Start's own server-fn compiler plugin knows how to strip for the client). Two server-only dependencies inside `callAiSquaredBoltConnector()` were getting dragged into the client bundle as a result: (a) a dynamic `import("@tanstack/react-start/server")` for `getCookie` — even though it's a runtime `await import()` inside an `async function`, Rollup still resolves and includes dynamic-import targets in the module graph while building it, before any reachability/dead-code analysis could remove it (confirmed live: a plain `import.meta.env.SSR` guard around the call did **not** fix the build on its own); (b) a dynamic `import("@/server/authCookie")` for the `APP_AUTH_COOKIE_NAME` constant — that base-owned file itself has a *static* top-level `import { deleteCookie, setCookie } from "@tanstack/react-start/server"`, so even after silencing Vite's analysis of the *first* import, this second one still pulled the same SSR-only code in transitively. Fixed both: added `/* @vite-ignore */` to the `@tanstack/react-start/server` dynamic import (tells Vite to skip static analysis of that one import expression, leaving it as a genuine runtime `import()` the server's real Bun/Node runtime resolves normally, never touched by the client bundler); replaced the dynamic `@/server/authCookie` import with the cookie name inlined as a local constant (`"ais-auth-cookie"`, copied verbatim from the base file's own value) — same reasoning already used elsewhere in this exact file for `AI_SQUARED_BOLT_CONNECTOR_ID` ("kept as a separate constant here since this file can't import client-side code").
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. The inlined cookie-name string must stay in sync with `/base/src/server/authCookie.ts`'s `APP_AUTH_COOKIE_NAME` if the base template ever changes that value (extremely unlikely, since it's a stable public API of the base) — verified byte-for-byte equal before inlining it.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This class of bug (server-only code leaking into the client bundle via a plain function reachable from a client-imported module) can recur elsewhere if a future change adds a new plain top-level function — not wrapped in `createServerFn` — that both (a) does something server-only (reads cookies/env secrets/Node APIs) and (b) lives in a file that a client-side hook/component also statically imports from. The general prevention pattern demonstrated here: either keep server-only logic entirely inside an actual `.handler()` closure (letting the framework's own compiler strip it), or isolate genuinely-shared helpers' server-only imports behind `/* @vite-ignore */` dynamic imports and avoid importing anything (even dynamically) whose own module has a *static* server-only import.
5. **Validation**: Reproduced the exact reported deploy failure locally by running `bun run build` directly (this project's rules otherwise avoid running it routinely) — confirmed the same `"Readable" is not exported by "__vite-browser-external"` error. Bisected the cause by temporarily stubbing out `callAiSquaredBoltConnector()`'s body entirely (confirmed the build passes with it stubbed, isolating the function), then narrowed it down import-by-import (confirmed the `@/server/authCookie` import specifically, not `getCookie` itself, was the remaining blocker after the `/* @vite-ignore */` fix alone). After both fixes, ran a full `bun run build` end-to-end successfully (`✓ built in ~31s`, `.output/` generated, `Generated .output/nitro.json`). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.62.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Theme Generator: Badges and Cards design tabs, Surprise Me randomizes both

1. **Goal / behavior**: Two new tabs on `/theme`'s Step 2 customization panel let a theme redesign the app's "bubbles" (Badges) and Cards app-wide while active. **Badges** tab: Shape (Pill/Rounded/Square), Fill (Solid/Soft Tint/Outline, using the theme's first accent color), an always-show-border toggle, and a background-opacity slider, with a live preview and a one-click reset. **Cards** tab: the pre-existing Corner Radius/Shadow Intensity sliders were moved here (out of Advanced) alongside new Fill (Solid/Glass/Soft Tint), Border (None/Subtle/Accent), and background-opacity controls, also with a live preview and reset. "Surprise Me" now independently rolls a ~50% chance each to randomize badge design and card design (leaving them on "App default" the other half), on top of its existing background/dark-variant randomization.
2. **Data flow**: `custom_themes` gained 4 badge columns (`badge_shape`, `badge_fill`, `badge_border`, `badge_opacity`) and 3 card columns (`card_fill`, `card_border`, `card_opacity`) via migrations `0022_many_blockbuster.sql`/`0023_short_leopardon.sql` — all nullable, same null-means-app-default convention as the pre-existing `radius`/`shadowStrength`. `src/lib/customTheme.ts` — new `BadgeShape`/`BadgeFill`/`CardFill`/`CardBorder` types, label maps, and `computeBadgePreviewStyle()`/`computeCardPreviewStyle()` (pure functions mirroring the CSS rules below, used for the builder's own live preview via inline `style` props so the preview doesn't depend on the global `custom-theme-active` class). `themeService.ts`'s `CustomThemeInput` and `createCustomTheme` gained the 7 fields (defaulted to `null` server-side). `ThemeApplier.tsx` sets `data-badge-shape`/`data-badge-fill`/`data-badge-border`/`--badge-opacity` and `data-card-fill`/`data-card-border`/`--card-opacity` on `<html>` only when the active theme actually sets them (removed/cleaned up on deactivate or theme change, same pattern as `--theme-anim-name`). `styles.css` — new attribute-selector rules target `[data-slot="badge"]`/`[data-slot="card"]` respectively; the card border/fill rules are placed *after* the pre-existing `.custom-theme-active [data-slot="card"]` frosted-glass rule so an explicit choice always wins over the default translucent look, while a card with no fields set keeps that default unchanged. `theme.tsx` — new state for all 7 fields, wired into `handleSave`/`handleDuplicate`/`handleResetBuilder`/`handleSurpriseMe`, plus the two new `TabsContent` panels (`badges`, `cards` — the latter absorbing the pre-existing radius/shadow sliders, removed from `advanced`).
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Opacity sliders are disabled/hinted-off until a Fill is actually chosen (badges: disabled specifically for "Outline" fill, which has no background to be transparent; cards: disabled until any Fill is picked).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Card "Border: None" uses `box-shadow: none !important` to fully flatten a card (deliberately overriding the existing shadow-opacity CSS variable rule, since "no border" is meant to be a decisive flat-design choice) — this is the one `!important` in the new rules, scoped to that single explicit user choice. Badge/card fill colors for "Solid"/"Soft"/"Glass" always derive from the theme's first accent color (or a neutral fallback) — there's no independent color picker specific to badges/cards, by design, matching how accent colors already work everywhere else in this theme system.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `custom_themes` column count increased twice, no new tables). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files. Confirmed `/theme` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added three Changelog entries (v2.64.0 Badges, v2.65.0 Surprise Me badges, and this Cards addition) and matching To-Do items, all marked done.

### Theme Generator: "Design For Me" curated-palette button (distinct from "Surprise Me")

1. **Goal / behavior**: A new "Design For Me" button sits next to "Surprise Me" on `/theme`. Unlike Surprise Me (which independently randomizes every field and can produce clashing colors), Design For Me generates a single cohesive, intentionally-designed-looking theme: one random base hue drives the whole palette via a color-harmony rule (analogous/complementary/triadic), background/overlay/accent colors are derived from it within hand-tuned saturation/lightness bands (never fully random hex), and badge/card styling is always set to a matching cohesive look (glass cards with a subtle border, soft-tint pill badges) rather than sometimes left unset.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `src/lib/customTheme.ts` — new `hslToHex(h, s, l)` pure conversion helper. `theme.tsx` — new `handleDesignForMe()`: picks a random base hue + a harmony offset (30°/180°/120°), derives `gradStart`/`gradEnd`/`overlayColor` from it at fixed dark/rich lightness levels, three accent colors at a fixed vivid saturation/lightness, a curated gradient direction and animation (Float/Pulse/Drift only — Aurora deliberately excluded as too visually busy for a "designed" look), and always sets Corner Radius/Shadow/Card Fill+Border+Opacity/Badge Shape+Fill+Opacity to fixed cohesive values (never left at "app default" the way Surprise Me sometimes randomly does). Reuses the existing `randomThemeName()` for the name and the same `setX` state setters `handleSurpriseMe` already uses — no new state was needed.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Purely client-side generation logic, same as Surprise Me.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: "Cohesive"/"pleasing" here means color-harmony math (complementary/analogous/triadic hue relationships) plus fixed saturation/lightness/effect ranges tuned by hand — it's a deterministic design heuristic, not an AI-generated or ML-scored aesthetic judgment. Dark Mode Variant is always left off by this button (Surprise Me can add one) to keep the generator simple and predictable.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across both touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on both touched files. Confirmed `/theme` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.68.0) and a To-Do item documenting the new button, marked done.

### Theme Generator: "Design For Me" now asks which sections to include

1. **Goal / behavior**: Clicking "Design For Me" now opens a small dialog first, listing Background (always included, shown checked and disabled), Effects, Badges, Cards, and Advanced: Dark Mode Variant as checkboxes (Effects/Badges/Cards default checked, Dark Mode Variant defaults unchecked) — only the checked sections get generated; an unchecked section is left exactly as it currently is in the builder rather than being reset or randomized.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `theme.tsx` — `handleDesignForMe()` now takes an `options: { effects, badges, cards, darkVariant }` argument; the background/accent-color generation block is unconditional (matches "Background is always used/enabled"), while the Effects (blur/darkness/noise/animation), Cards (radius/shadow/fill/border/opacity), Badges (shape/fill/border/opacity), and new Dark Mode Variant (dark background/overlay colors, derived from the same base hue) blocks are each wrapped in an `if (options.x)`. New `designForMeOpen`/`designOptions` state and a `Dialog` (using the newly-imported `Checkbox` component) sit between the existing Gallery-picker dialog and the closing page `<div>`; the toolbar button now opens the dialog instead of calling the generator directly, and the dialog's "Generate" button calls `handleDesignForMe(designOptions)` then closes itself.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The Dark Mode Variant option, when checked, always turns the toggle on and fills in a derived dark background/overlay (there's no "leave dark variant as-is but change nothing" middle state) — same all-or-nothing granularity the other three checkboxes have.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Confirmed `/theme` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.69.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: "Design For Me" dialog wasn't closing; added a Light/Dark Tone option

1. **Goal / behavior**: (a) The "Design For Me" dialog's Cancel and Generate buttons (and the X close icon) now reliably close the dialog. (b) The dialog gained a "Tone" control (Darker/Lighter, defaulting to Darker) that shifts the whole generated palette's lightness band brighter or moodier.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. (a) Root cause: the last checkbox row's Base UI accessibility hit-area (`after:-inset-y-2`, an invisible ~8px hit-target expansion baked into the shared `Checkbox` component) sat directly above the Cancel/Generate button row with only an 8px `pt-2` gap, so clicks intended for those buttons were sometimes intercepted by the checkbox's invisible extended hit area instead. Fixed by restructuring the dialog body into a single `gap-4` flex column (checkboxes group, a `Separator`, the new Tone control, then the button row with its own `mt-2` on top of that gap) — comfortably more vertical distance than the 8px overlap — and, as a second layer of robustness, switched Cancel to the primitive `DialogClose` component (`render={<Button variant="outline" />}`, the same pattern `dialog.tsx`'s own built-in X button uses) instead of a manual `onClick={() => setDesignForMeOpen(false)}`, and reordered Generate's handler to call `setDesignForMeOpen(false)` *before* `handleDesignForMe(...)` so a future exception inside the generator can never block the dialog from closing. The disabled "Background" checkbox also gained a no-op `onCheckedChange={() => {}}` to avoid it being an unhandled controlled input. (b) `handleDesignForMe`'s `options` gained a `tone: "dark" | "light"` field; two hand-tuned lightness arrays (`bgL`/`accentL`) are selected by tone and used everywhere the background/overlay/accent HSL lightness values were previously hardcoded — "Lighter" is still a comparatively dark, glass-appropriate band (not a true light-mode background), just noticeably softer than "Darker". New `designOptions.tone` state (default `"dark"`) and a `ToggleGroup` (Darker/Lighter) in the dialog, placed after a `Separator` below the section checkboxes.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The checkbox hit-area overlap is a general Base UI `Checkbox` characteristic (same component used everywhere in this app) — any future dialog stacking a checkbox immediately above interactive controls with less than ~12-16px of gap could reproduce the same symptom; the fix here is spacing/robustness at the call site, not a change to the shared component (which is base-owned and read-only). Tone only affects background/overlay/accent lightness — Effects/Badges/Cards' own fixed values (opacity, shape, fill, etc.) are unaffected by the tone choice.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Confirmed `/theme` still returns 200 from the dev server. Playwright browser verification was unavailable in this environment (Chromium not installed) — relied on identifying and fixing the concrete layout/robustness issue directly in code instead, per project rules for environment/browser errors. Added a Changelog entry (v2.70.0) and a To-Do item documenting both the fix and the new Tone option, marked done.

### Fix: "Design For Me" dialog still not closing — switched to conditional mounting

1. **Goal / behavior**: The previous fix (checkbox hit-area spacing + `DialogClose`) did not resolve the reported issue — Generate/Cancel/the X icon still failed to close the dialog for the user. Reassessed rather than repeating variations: the dialog is now only mounted in the React tree while `designForMeOpen` is true, so React itself unconditionally tears down the whole dialog subtree the instant that state flips to `false` — this no longer depends on Base UI's internal open/close store plumbing, its close-button click handler, or its exit-animation/`mounted`-state bookkeeping completing correctly.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `theme.tsx` — the dialog's JSX is now wrapped in `{designForMeOpen && (<Dialog open onOpenChange={(next) => { if (!next) setDesignForMeOpen(false); }}>...)}` instead of always being rendered with `open={designForMeOpen}`; `Dialog`'s own `open` prop is now the constant `true` (meaningful only while the block exists at all) rather than reading the boolean directly, so a stray internal store desync can no longer leave a "closed" dialog still visually mounted. Both the Cancel and Generate buttons now call `setDesignForMeOpen(false)` directly as a plain `onClick` (also added explicit `type="button"` to both, defensively ruling out any implicit-submit interference) instead of Cancel going through the `DialogClose` primitive component (removed, along with its now-unused import) — removing one more layer of indirection between "user clicks" and "state actually changes."
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The dialog no longer plays its close/exit animation (fade-out/zoom-out) since the whole subtree unmounts immediately rather than waiting for a CSS transition — an intentional tradeoff prioritizing "it definitely closes" over the cosmetic close animation; the open/entry animation is unaffected. If a dialog elsewhere in this app is ever reported with the same "won't close" symptom, this conditional-mount pattern (mount only while a boolean is true, rather than passing that boolean straight through as `open`) is the fix to reach for first, ahead of spacing/handler-plumbing changes.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Confirmed `/theme` still returns 200 from the dev server. Playwright browser verification was unavailable in this environment (Chromium not installed) — per project rules, relied on switching to a structurally different, dependency-free closing mechanism (conditional mount) instead of further tweaking the previous (still-dependent-on-the-primitive) approach. Added a Changelog entry (v2.70.1) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Improvement: Data Dashboard connects to any data source, not just one hardcoded connector

1. **Goal / behavior**: `/data-dashboard` previously always queried a single hardcoded connector (id 2108, "Supabase Connector App Gen"). It now shows a "Data Source" connector picker that lists every connected data-source connector, defaulting to the first one; the user can switch sources at any time. The table picker underneath still lists that connector's live schema and lets the user choose a table exactly as before. If no data-source connectors are configured, the page shows an empty state instead of erroring.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `data-dashboard.tsx` — added `useConnectors({ type: "source", category: "data", per_page: 100 })` to fetch the list of available source connectors; a `Select` lets the user pick one (state defaults to the first connector once the list loads); the existing table-discovery/query logic (previously always targeting connector id 2108) now runs against whichever connector id is currently selected, re-fetching the schema/table list whenever the selection changes.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. No connector id is hardcoded anymore.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Only connectors of type `source` and category `data` are offered — AI/ML model connectors and destination connectors are intentionally excluded from this picker, consistent with this page's purpose (browsing tabular data). Switching the data source resets the selected table, since a different connector's schema will generally not contain the same table names.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` showed no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` was run on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.71.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Improvement: Data Dashboard's connector picker excludes unstructured document connectors

1. **Goal / behavior**: `/data-dashboard`'s "Data Source" connector picker (added in the entry above) no longer lists unstructured document connectors (e.g. OneDrive's Unstructured mode) — only structured/tabular data source connectors are selectable, since this page's table-discovery logic (`information_schema` column discovery + `SELECT * FROM <table>`) needs a real SQL schema that unstructured connectors don't provide. The empty state now reads "No structured data source connectors available".
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `data-dashboard.tsx` — the existing `useConnectors({ type: "source", category: "data", per_page: 100 })` result is now further filtered with `.filter((c) => c.attributes.configuration?.data_type !== "unstructured")` before being used to populate the picker/select-first-connector logic, mirroring the same filter already used on `/insights`'s Data Source picker.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Same scope limits as the pre-existing `/insights` filter — a connector is only excluded when its `configuration.data_type` is exactly `"unstructured"`; any future connector type that also lacks a queryable SQL schema but doesn't set this flag would not be caught by this filter.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` run on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.72.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: Data Dashboard 422'd for non-SQL "structured" connectors

1. **Goal / behavior**: `/data-dashboard` was failing with "Couldn't load live data: AIS API request failed with status 422" whenever the selected connector was a structured-but-non-SQL type (e.g. Salesforce, QuickBooks, Odoo, SFTP, OneDrive-Structured, Qdrant, Pinecone). The previous filter (excluding only `configuration.data_type === "unstructured"`) still let these through, but the page always ran a fixed `SELECT table_name FROM information_schema.tables WHERE table_schema = 'public'` query that only real SQL engines understand. The connector picker now only lists true SQL-queryable connectors, and table/row discovery uses each engine's own correct catalog query.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. `data-dashboard.tsx` — the connector filter now checks `!!DISCOVERY_QUERIES[c.attributes.connector_name]` (imported from `@/components/connectors/ConnectorDetailSheet`, the same allow-list map already used on the Connectors and AI Insights pages: Postgresql, MariaDB, Oracle, Bigquery, Snowflake, Databricks, Clickhouse, AwsAthena) instead of the old `data_type !== "unstructured"` check. Table discovery now runs the connector's own `DISCOVERY_QUERIES[connectorProvider]` column-level catalog query (the same one the Connectors/Insights pages use for schema introspection) and derives the distinct list of table names from its result set, rather than querying `information_schema.tables` directly. Row queries (`SELECT * FROM <table> LIMIT 200`) now build the qualified/quoted table reference with a `quoteIdent` helper (backticks for BigQuery, double quotes for every other engine) instead of assuming double-quote Postgres-style quoting.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Same allow-list boundary as the Connectors/Insights pages — a connector engine only appears if it has an entry in `DISCOVERY_QUERIES`; any future SQL-queryable connector type added to the platform needs an entry there before it will show up on this page too. Non-SQL connectors (Salesforce, QuickBooks, Odoo, SFTP, OneDrive, Qdrant, Pinecone, etc.) are excluded entirely rather than degraded to a partial experience.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` showed no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` was run on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.73.0) and a To-Do item documenting the issue and the fix, marked done.

### Fix: Secrets page dialog wouldn't close

1. **Goal / behavior**: The Secrets & API Keys page's ("New Secret" dialog would not close when clicking Cancel, Create Secret, or Done. Reused the same proven fix already applied to the Theme Generator's "Design For Me" dialog (see "Fix: 'Design For Me' dialog still not closing — switched to conditional mounting" above) rather than trying new variations: the dialog is now only mounted in the React tree while `createOpen` is true, so React itself unconditionally tears down the whole dialog subtree the instant that state flips to `false`, instead of depending on the Base UI dialog primitive's internal open/close store or animation lifecycle.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `secrets.tsx` — the dialog's `DialogContent` (covering both the "Secret created" success view and the "Create a new secret" form view) is now wrapped in `{createOpen && (<DialogContent>...)}` instead of always being rendered while only `Dialog`'s own `open={createOpen}` prop controlled visibility. Also added explicit `type="button"` to every button inside the dialog (Cancel, Create Secret, Done, Copy) as a defensive measure ruling out any implicit-submit interference, matching the same prior fix's approach.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Same tradeoff as the original fix — the dialog no longer plays its close/exit animation since the subtree unmounts immediately rather than waiting for a CSS transition; the open/entry animation is unaffected.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` showed no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` errors only). `bunx biome format --write` was run on the touched file. Playwright browser verification was not attempted (Chromium unavailable in this environment, per project convention) — instead the same proven code pattern from the Theme Generator fix was reused directly rather than re-deriving and re-testing a new approach. Added a Changelog entry (v2.74.0, id 126) and a To-Do item (id 200) documenting the issue and the fix, marked done.

### New page: Brand & Asset Library (`/brand-assets`)

1. **Goal / behavior**: A new page (Files & Media nav group, placed after "Export Center") for managing brand assets — logos, icons, color palettes, fonts, templates, and other files. Assets are shown as a grid of cards with image thumbnails (fetched via presigned URL for image content types, an icon placeholder for everything else), a category badge, version/size, tags, and a notes preview. A toolbar provides search by name/tag and a category filter dropdown, plus an "Upload Asset" dialog (file picker + name/category/version/tags/notes fields). Per-card actions: Download (opens the presigned URL), Edit (metadata-only dialog), and Delete (`AlertDialog` confirmation, removes both the database row and the underlying S3 file).
2. **Data flow**: New `brand_assets` table in `src/server/db/schema.ts` (`name`, `category` — `"logo" | "icon" | "color_palette" | "font" | "template" | "other"`, default `"other"` — nullable `version`, nullable `tags` text array, nullable `notes`, `storageFileId` integer NOT NULL referencing `storage_files.id` with `onDelete: "cascade"`, `createdAt`, `updatedAt`), migrated via `db:generate`/`db:migrate` (migration `0025_red_blacklash.sql`). New `src/services/db/brandAssetService.ts`: `listBrandAssets` joins `brand_assets` with `storage_files` for file metadata (size, content type, S3 key); `createBrandAsset` accepts `FormData` (file + name/category/version/tags/notes), uploads the file to the `assets/` S3 folder via the existing `uploadFile` helper, inserts a `storage_files` row, then a `brand_assets` row; `updateBrandAsset` edits metadata only (no file replacement); `deleteBrandAsset` deletes the `brand_assets` row, then the `storage_files` row and the underlying S3 object via `deleteFile`. `brand-assets.tsx` is a standard `useQuery`/`useMutation` page gated on `useCapabilities()` requiring **both** `databaseEnabled` and `s3Enabled` (this page uses Database and Storage together), with loading/not-configured/empty states; every create/update/delete mutation logs via `useActivityLog().logAudit` under category `"brand_assets"`. Dialogs use the app's established conditional-mounting pattern (`{createOpen && (<DialogContent>...)}`) to avoid the known Base UI dialog-won't-close issue documented in earlier entries above.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL` and the already-provisioned S3 storage. Registered in `src/components/layout/index.ts` under the existing "Files & Media" nav group (title "Brand & Asset Library", url `/brand-assets`, icon `Layers` from `lucide-react`), placed after "Export Center".
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Editing an asset only changes its metadata (name/category/version/tags/notes) — there is no "replace file" action; uploading a new version requires creating a new asset entry. Like other Database+Storage pages in this app, the page is unusable (shows a not-configured state) unless both capabilities are enabled — there is no partial/metadata-only fallback mode.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `brand_assets` appeared in the table list with 9 columns and 1 fk). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` showed no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` errors only). `bunx biome format --write` was run on all touched/created files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.75.0, id 127) and a To-Do item (id 201) documenting the new page, marked done.

### Fix: Brand & Asset Library image cards showed an icon instead of a real preview

1. **Goal / behavior**: Image-type assets on `/brand-assets` now show a real thumbnail preview (with a loading `Skeleton` while the presigned URL fetches, then an "Image" badge overlay), matching the Theme Generator gallery card's preview style, instead of falling back to a plain category icon while the URL was loading.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `brand-assets.tsx`'s `AssetThumbnail` — while `isImage && isLoading`, renders a `Skeleton`; once loaded, renders the image as a CSS `background: url(...) center/contain no-repeat` div (mirroring `theme.tsx`'s `GeneratedThemeCard`) with an "Image" `Badge` overlay, instead of an `<img>` tag shown only after the query settled.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Non-image assets (fonts/templates/palettes/other) still show the category icon placeholder, unchanged.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.75.1, id 128) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Feature: use a logo from the Brand & Asset Library as the app's logo

1. **Goal / behavior**: Logo-category image assets on `/brand-assets` gained a "Set as active logo" / "Remove as active logo" toggle button plus an "Active App Logo" badge on the currently-selected one. The sidebar header (every page) now renders that logo image in place of the generic `Hexagon` icon whenever one is set; with none set, the sidebar shows the original icon unchanged.
2. **Data flow**: New singleton `app_branding` table (`id` fixed at `1`, nullable `activeLogoAssetId` FK → `brand_assets.id` with `onDelete: "set null"`, `updatedAt`) in `src/server/db/schema.ts`, migrated via `db:generate`/`db:migrate` (migration `0026_handy_virginia_dare.sql`) — mirrors the existing `active_themes` singleton-row pattern used by the Theme Generator. New `src/services/db/brandingService.ts`: `getActiveLogo()` (left/inner-joins `app_branding` → `brand_assets` → `storage_files`, returns `{assetId, name, fileKey, contentType}` or `null`), `setActiveLogo(assetId)`/`clearActiveLogo()` (both `insert ... onConflictDoUpdate` against the fixed `id: 1` row). `brand-assets.tsx` — new `activeLogo` query plus `setActiveLogoMut`/`clearActiveLogoMut`; the toggle button only renders for assets whose `category === "logo"` and whose `contentType` starts with `image/`. `app-sidebar.tsx` — new `useCapabilities()` + `getActiveLogo()` + `getStorageFileUrl()` queries (gated on `databaseEnabled && s3Enabled`); the header's icon box renders the resolved logo `<img>` when present, else the original `Hexagon` icon.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL` and the already-provisioned S3 storage. Per the user's explicit choice, only the sidebar header logo was wired up (not the sign-in page), and the active logo is chosen manually from the library (not auto-picked as "most recent upload").
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Only one logo can be active app-wide at a time (no per-page override, unlike the theme system's per-page scoping). The button is restricted to `category === "logo"` image assets — a non-"logo"-category image asset (e.g. an icon or template image) can't be set as the active logo without first re-categorizing it via Edit. If both `databaseEnabled`/`s3Enabled` aren't true, the sidebar silently keeps the default icon (query is simply disabled) rather than showing an error.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `app_branding` in the table list). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all three touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new error from referencing a nonexistent `storageFiles.fileKey` column instead of the actual `storageFiles.key`). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched/created files. Confirmed `/brand-assets` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.76.0, id 129) and a To-Do item documenting the feature, marked done.

### Fix: AI Chat's "AI Squared Bolt" option repointed at a working connector

1. **Goal / behavior**: The "AI Squared Bolt" option in AI Chat previously failed every time with a 401 (see the two prior entries above documenting that connector id `1803` wasn't authorized for model execution on this workspace). Re-verified live via the `aisquared` MCP tools that a different, currently-connected AI/ML connector — id `2342`, "App Builder AI Squared Bolt" — successfully executes and returns real completions, and repointed the option at it. Note connector `1803` also now returns a successful completion when tested directly via the MCP tools, so the underlying platform authorization issue may have been resolved workspace-wide; `2342` was chosen specifically because it's the connector attached/pinned for this app.
2. **Data flow**: No new tables/services. Single shared constant `AI_SQUARED_BOLT_CONNECTOR_ID` changed from `1803` to `2342` in both `src/lib/aiProviders.ts` (client-safe constant used by `useAiModel.ts`/`chat.tsx`) and the duplicated server-only copy in `src/services/ai/aiModelService.ts` (kept duplicated rather than imported, for the same client/server bundling reason documented in that file's comments). No other code changed — `useAiModel()`'s dispatch, the 401 detection (`isUnauthorized()`), and the `aiSquaredBoltUnavailableMessage()` fallback toast in `chat.tsx` are all unchanged and still apply if this connector's authorization is ever revoked again.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This only fixes AI Chat's UI-driven path (`useExecuteModel()` → tRPC `connectors.executeModel`). It hasn't been re-verified whether the live app session (as opposed to the MCP tool's own auth) actually succeeds end-to-end through the chat UI — the MCP tool confirming the connector itself works is a strong signal but a different auth path than the deployed app's own session cookie. If a 401 recurs through the app itself, the existing fallback message will still surface it clearly.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified both connector `2342` and `1803` via `aisquared_connectors_execute_model`, both returning real completions (model `aisquared/bolt-instruct-32b`). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on both touched files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.77.0, id 130) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `app_branding` in the table list). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all three touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new error from referencing a nonexistent `storageFiles.fileKey` column instead of the actual `storageFiles.key`). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched/created files. Confirmed `/brand-assets` still returns 200 from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.76.0, id 129) and a To-Do item documenting the feature, marked done.

### Fix: AI Chat error when using "AI Squared Bolt" (connector-config dependency removed)

1. **Goal / behavior**: Using "AI Squared Bolt" in AI Chat produced a generic error (not a 401 this time). Root cause: the platform's `connectors.executeModel` procedure, when called with `{ connectorId, messages, instructions }`, first fetches the connector and runs `buildLlmPayload()` against its own stored `configuration.request_format` field — which throws `"Missing request_format in connector configuration for Aisquared"` if that field is missing/not valid JSON on whichever connector is configured. This is a property of how a given connector happens to be set up on the platform, independent of whether it can run a model at all — likely bit the app right after the previous fix repointed the option at connector `2342` without that connector's own `request_format` having been verified.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `useAiModel.ts` — new `buildAiSquaredBoltPayload(messages, instructions)` helper (merges `instructions` in as a leading system message); the "ai-squared-bolt" branches of `mutate()`/`mutateAsync()` now call `connectorExec.mutate/mutateAsync({ connectorId, payload: buildAiSquaredBoltPayload(...) })` — the `{ connectorId, payload }` variant of `useExecuteModel()`'s input — instead of `{ connectorId, messages, instructions }`. That variant is forwarded straight to the platform's `execute_model` endpoint, skipping the connector-GET-and-config-build step entirely. `src/services/ai/aiModelService.ts`'s `callAiSquaredBoltConnector()` (the server-side fallback used by Document Pipeline) got the identical treatment — dropped its manual `GET connector` + `buildLlmPayload()` call and now builds the same plain `{ messages }` payload directly.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This payload shape (`{ messages }`, `instructions` as a leading system message) is specific to how the "Aisquared" connector type's `execute_model` endpoint behaves (confirmed live) — if the app ever needs to support a different connector *type* for this same code path (e.g. Anthropic/OpenAI-backed), the payload shape would need to branch again, since providers other than Aisquared expect different fields (e.g. `model`, `max_tokens`, `system`). Not needed today since `AI_SQUARED_BOLT_CONNECTOR_ID` is always an "Aisquared"-type connector.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified via `aisquared_connectors_execute_model` against connector `2342` using the exact payload shape the new code now sends (`{ messages: [{role: "system", ...}, {role: "user", ...}] }`) — returned a real completion (model `aisquared/bolt-instruct-32b`). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across both touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on both touched files. Playwright browser verification was not attempted (Chromium unavailable in this environment, per project convention); relied on the dev server's own error log (which confirmed no unrelated regressions) plus the live platform-tool verification above instead. Added a Changelog entry (v2.78.0, id 131) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: AI Chat errors caused the page to reset instead of showing what went wrong

1. **Goal / behavior**: When an AI request in AI Chat fails (for any reason — connector auth, network, a bad response shape, or a failure while saving the assistant's reply), the failure now always shows as a clear message inside the conversation itself, and the page no longer appears to reload/redirect to the Dashboard. Per an explicit request, this app also gained its first ever TanStack Router `errorComponent` (on the `/chat` route only) so a genuine render-time exception is caught and displayed in place, inside the normal sidebar/header layout, instead of a blank screen.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. Root cause: `handleSend`'s `onAiSuccess`/`onAiError` callbacks (passed to `aiModel.mutate(...)`, a TanStack Query mutation) had no try/catch of their own, and TanStack Query does not catch exceptions thrown inside consumer-supplied `onSuccess`/`onError` callbacks — any throw there becomes an unhandled rejection with nothing shown to the user. Two concrete gaps existed: `onAiSuccess`'s `addMessage(...).then(...)` chain (persisting the assistant's reply) had no `.catch`, so a save failure there was silently swallowed; and neither callback had any safety net at all if something inside them threw synchronously. `chat.tsx` — new `chatError: string | null` state (cleared on send/new-chat/conversation-switch) rendered as a destructive `Alert` inside the message pane (both the populated-messages branch and the previously message-hiding `uiMessages.length === 0` branch, adjusted to `&& !chatError` so a first-message failure in a brand-new conversation is still visible instead of being hidden behind the "Send a message..." placeholder). New `describeAiError(error, provider)` — a single, itself-defensive (wrapped in its own try/catch) helper producing the plain-language message for both the toast and the inline banner, replacing the inline if/else chain that used to live directly in `onAiError`. `onAiSuccess`/`onAiError` bodies are now each wrapped in their own `try/catch`, and the `addMessage(...)` promise chain now has a `.catch` that sets `chatError` and logs instead of leaving an unhandled rejection. New route option `errorComponent: ChatRouteError` (a small component using the existing `Empty`/`EmptyHeader`/`EmptyMedia` pattern already used elsewhere on this page, plus a "Reload chat" button) — the first `errorComponent` anywhere in this app's route tree (confirmed via a full `grep` that none existed before, on any route including `__root.tsx`).
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This makes failures visible and non-crashing; it does not itself change whether a given AI Squared Bolt/Anthropic/OpenAI call succeeds — an actual platform/API-side failure will still fail, just with a clear message in the conversation instead of a silent or page-resetting one. The new `errorComponent` only covers the `/chat` route — no other route in the app has one; if the same "render error causes a reset" symptom is ever reported on a different page, the fix here (try/catch the mutation callbacks + a route `errorComponent`) is the pattern to reuse.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Playwright browser verification was unavailable in this environment (Chromium not installed) — relied on tracing the actual TanStack Query callback-exception-handling gap in the code (confirmed via reading TanStack Query's own callback-invocation behavior and this file's exact call sites) rather than guessing, per project rules for environment/browser errors. Added a Changelog entry (v2.79.0, id 132) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Fix: found the real cause of the AI Chat "redirected to Dashboard" — a global auth-error handler, not a rendering bug

1. **Goal / behavior**: The previous fix (inline error banner + a `/chat` `errorComponent`) did not resolve the reported redirect, because the actual cause had nothing to do with rendering or React error boundaries at all. AI Chat (and any other page that calls `connectors.*`/`workflows.*`) no longer gets hard-navigated away when a connector call fails with a permission error — the page's own existing inline error handling is what the user now sees.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/UI change on `chat.tsx` itself this time — the fix is entirely in `src/router.tsx`. Root cause: `getRouter()`'s `QueryClient` has a global `QueryCache`/`MutationCache` `onError` (`handleTrpcAuthError`) that, on ANY tRPC error with code `UNAUTHORIZED`, calls `window.location.assign("/sign-in")` — a real hard browser navigation, not a React state change, so it completely bypasses every route's own error handling, including a per-route `errorComponent` (confirmed: `errorComponent` only catches errors thrown during that route's own render/loader inside the router's reconciliation; it has no relationship to `QueryCache`/`MutationCache`-level `onError`, which fires independently and in addition to any query/mutation's own local `onSuccess`/`onError`). Separately, base's `platformRequest`/`enterpriseApiFetch` (used by the `connectors`/`workflows` tRPC routers) map **any** upstream 401 from the platform's own API — including a connector-specific model-execution permission gate, the exact class of error documented extensively in the AI Squared Bolt entries above — to the same generic tRPC `UNAUTHORIZED` code used for "your app session itself is invalid." `handleTrpcAuthError` had no way to tell those two cases apart, so a connector 401 on `/chat` triggered a hard redirect to `/sign-in`, which (on this app's default public/no-login mode) immediately redirects again to `/` via `__root.tsx`'s own `beforeLoad` — the "redirected to Dashboard" the user kept seeing, and a fresh full-page load, not a soft in-app navigation, which is also why it looked like "the app reloaded." Fixed by adding `AUTH_REDIRECT_PROCEDURE_NAMESPACES` (`"auth"`, `"capabilities"`) — `handleTrpcAuthError` now inspects the erroring query's/mutation's own tRPC procedure path (via a new `trpcProcedurePath()` helper reading `query.queryKey`/`mutation.options.mutationKey`, whose first element is the tRPC path array per `@trpc/tanstack-react-query`'s own key-building code) and only hard-redirects when that path's first segment is in the allow-list; any other namespace (`connectors`, `workflows`, etc.) falls through and lets the page's own existing local error handling (already present on `/chat`, `/insights`, `/query`, `/workflows`, `/document-pipeline`, `/data-explorer`, `/connectors` per multiple entries above) show its own message instead.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. `AUTH_REDIRECT_PROCEDURE_NAMESPACES` is a small hardcoded allow-list matching this app's actual own-session-auth-related tRPC routers (`auth`, `capabilities`) — any future tRPC router that's genuinely session-auth-gated (not a platform-passthrough) would need adding to this set to keep getting the hard-redirect treatment; anything not in the set defaults to "don't redirect" (falls through to local handling) except when the query/mutation key shape can't be parsed at all, which conservatively still redirects (preserving the previous behavior for anything unexpected).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This fixes the *symptom that made connector-level errors unrecoverable/confusing* — it does not and cannot fix the underlying platform-side "AI Squared Bolt" model-execution permission gate itself (documented across several entries above as something only the platform/workspace side can grant). A genuine app-session expiry (e.g. an actually invalid/expired cookie) still correctly hard-redirects to sign-in, since `auth`/`capabilities` calls remain in the allow-list. If a *different* tRPC router is added later that's genuinely session-gated (not a platform passthrough) and expected to trigger this same redirect-on-401 behavior, it needs to be added to `AUTH_REDIRECT_PROCEDURE_NAMESPACES` — otherwise a real session failure on that router would silently just show as a generic error instead of redirecting.
5. **Validation**: Traced the exact mechanism by reading `@trpc/tanstack-react-query`'s own `getQueryKeyInternal`/`getMutationKeyInternal` source (confirming the tRPC path is reliably the first element of `queryKey`/`mutationKey`) and `/base/src/server/platform/request.ts`'s `handleErrorResponse` (confirming it maps every upstream 401, unconditionally, to `TRPCError({code: "UNAUTHORIZED"})` with no distinction for *why* the platform returned 401) before writing the fix, rather than guessing. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Playwright browser verification was unavailable in this environment (Chromium not installed) — relied on tracing the real navigation mechanism (`window.location.assign`, a genuine browser API call, confirmed by reading the file directly) instead of guessing at render-level causes a second time, per project rules for environment/browser errors. Added a Changelog entry (v2.80.0, id 133) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Moved the floating theme toggle/badge and global search button into the header nav
