1. **Goal / behavior**: The light/dark toggle + "current theme" badge (previously a floating pill fixed to the top-right of every page) and the global page-search button (previously a floating circular button fixed to the bottom-right) are now both rendered inline inside the app's top header bar, next to the existing Refresh and (unrelated, base-owned) app-wide dark-mode `ThemeToggle` buttons. The search button's look was also changed from a large floating circle to a compact ghost icon button matching the header's existing button sizing/style.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change — purely a component-placement and styling refactor. `ThemeLockIndicator.tsx` — removed its `fixed top-2 right-3 z-20` wrapper (now a plain `flex items-center gap-1`); the manual light/dark toggle switched from a hand-styled `<button>` to the shared `Button` component (`variant="ghost" size="icon" className="h-9 w-9"`, matching `RefreshButton`'s exact sizing) with `h-4 w-4` icons instead of `size-3.5`; the theme-status `<Link>` pill kept its rounded-border look but dropped the `shadow-sm backdrop-blur-md`/pill-shaped (`rounded-full`) floating styling for a flatter `rounded-md` inline pill with a `hover:bg-accent` state matching other header controls, and its max-width shrunk from `max-w-40` to `max-w-32` to fit the header's limited horizontal space. `GlobalSearch.tsx` — same component/logic (keyboard shortcut listener, `CommandDialog`), only the trigger `Button` changed from `variant="outline" ... fixed bottom-5 right-5 z-50 size-11 rounded-full ... shadow-lg` to `variant="ghost" size="icon" className="h-9 w-9"` with a `h-4 w-4` icon — now visually indistinguishable in style from the header's other icon buttons. `AppHeader.tsx` — both components imported and rendered inside the existing `ml-auto flex items-center gap-1` button group, ordered `GlobalSearch`, `ThemeLockIndicator`, `RefreshButton`, `ThemeToggle`. `_protected.tsx` — removed the two standalone `<GlobalSearch />`/`<ThemeLockIndicator />` mounts (both components are self-contained with their own hooks/queries, so no props needed to be threaded through `AppHeader`); `<ThemeApplier />` (the actual background/CSS-variable theme-application logic, unrelated to this UI relocation) is unchanged and still mounted there.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Both components are only rendered where `AppHeader` renders, i.e. inside `AppLayout`'s multi-page sidebar layout — this app already uses that layout for every page, so there's no coverage gap, but a future single-page (`AppShell`) section of the app would not automatically get these controls unless `AppShell` also renders `AppHeader` or these components are added there too. The theme-status pill's label truncates more aggressively now (`max-w-32` vs the previous floating pill's `max-w-40`) to fit the header's limited width — a very long theme name will truncate slightly earlier than before.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all four touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all four touched files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.81.0, id 134) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Audit: dialog/popup "doesn't close" bug — found and fixed the one real instance (Inventory)

1. **Goal / behavior**: Audited every `Dialog`/`AlertDialog`/`Sheet`/`Drawer`/`Popover`/`DropdownMenu` in the app (32 files) for the reported "there's always an issue when a popup is added where it doesn't close." Every dialog elsewhere in the app was already correctly wired (`open`/`onOpenChange` bound to real state, Cancel calling the same setter, outside-click/Escape working) — most already use this app's established fix for the dialog-primitive's own close-reliability quirk (conditional mounting, `{open && <DialogContent>...}`, documented in the Theme "Design For Me" and Secrets entries above). The one real bug found: Inventory's Add/Edit Item dialog *did* visually close on Cancel, but its `editingId`/`formData` state — which decides whether the dialog is in "Add" or "Edit" mode — was only ever reset inside the two mutations' `onSuccess` handlers, never on Cancel. Clicking Edit, then Cancel, then "Add Item" again silently reopened in Edit mode with the previous item's data still filled in, and submitting would update that old item instead of creating a new one.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `inventory.tsx` — the `Dialog`'s `onOpenChange` now calls `resetForm()` whenever it transitions to closed, for any reason (Cancel, outside click, Escape), not just the two mutations' own success paths (which still also call it directly, now redundantly-but-harmlessly). The dialog's `DialogContent` was also wrapped in `{open && (...)}` (previously always rendered, only `open` toggling visibility) to bring it in line with the project's proven conditional-mount pattern, and both footer buttons got explicit `type="button"` as the same defensive measure used in the Theme/Secrets fixes.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This audit covered every dialog-family component currently in the codebase (listed exhaustively during investigation) — no other instance of this "stale closed-state" or "won't actually close" pattern was found. If a *newly added* dialog in the future exhibits a similar symptom, the two things to check first (in order) are: (a) does every "controlling state" variable get reset in the close handler, not only on success, and (b) is `DialogContent`/`AlertDialogContent`/etc. conditionally mounted (`{open && <...>}`) rather than always rendered with only `open` toggling visibility.
5. **Validation**: Read all 32 files rendering a dialog-family component before concluding only one had a real bug (not a blind pattern-match). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.82.0, id 135) and a To-Do item documenting the audit and the fix, marked done.

### Fix: Global search popup still wouldn't close (missed by the dialog audit — uses `CommandDialog`, not `Dialog`)

1. **Goal / behavior**: The global page-search popup (header search icon / Cmd/Ctrl+K, `GlobalSearch.tsx`) now closes reliably on selecting a page, pressing Escape, or clicking outside — the previous app-wide dialog audit (entry above) missed this one because it's built on the shared `CommandDialog` component rather than a plain `Dialog`/`AlertDialog`, so it wasn't in the literal `<Dialog>`/`<AlertDialog>` grep sweep that audit was based on, even though `CommandDialog` (`/base/src/components/ui/command.tsx`) is just a thin wrapper around the exact same `Dialog`/`DialogContent` primitives that needed the conditional-mount fix everywhere else.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `GlobalSearch.tsx` — the `CommandDialog` (and everything inside it) is now wrapped in `{open && (...)}` instead of always being rendered with only its own `open`/`onOpenChange` props toggling visibility (same pattern as the Theme "Design For Me"/Secrets/Inventory fixes above); `CommandDialog`'s `open` prop is now the constant `true` (meaningful only while the block exists at all), and its `onOpenChange` calls `setOpen(false)` when told to close, mirroring the exact wiring used in those prior fixes.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Same tradeoff as the other conditional-mount fixes — the popup's close/exit animation no longer plays since the subtree unmounts immediately; the open/entry animation is unaffected. Any *other* component in this app built on `CommandDialog` (there are currently none besides this one) would need the same treatment if ever reported with the same symptom, since the underlying cause is in the shared primitive's lifecycle, not this component's own logic specifically.

### Four new pages: Media Library Search, Integration Health, Saved Reports, User Roles & Permissions

1. **Goal / behavior**: Four new pages, one per idea previously suggested for each nav section. **Media Library Search** (`/media-search`, Files & Media) — read-only unified search/filter (by name, tag, category, source, type) across Storage, Gallery, and the Brand & Asset Library, with each result card linking back to its home page. **Integration Health** (`/integration-health`, Platform) — lists every connector that supports a lightweight test query, a per-connector "Test" action that runs `SELECT 1` and records the result, summary counts (Healthy/Failing/Not tested), and a full check-history table with a Clear History action. **Saved Reports** (`/saved-reports`, Live Insights) — bookmark either a Data Explorer natural-language question or an AI Insights configuration (connector + tables) under a name, then re-run it on demand from one list; each card shows the last run time and a Markdown-rendered result preview. **User Roles & Permissions** (`/roles-permissions`, Configuration) — define named roles (with a color and description) and, per role, a checkbox matrix of every registered nav page — explicitly framed as a reference/documentation matrix (an `Alert` banner states this directly), not enforced access control, since this app's sign-in is platform-managed with no custom user/login table.
2. **Data flow**: Five new tables in `src/server/db/schema.ts`, migrated via `db:generate`/`db:migrate` (migration `0028_loving_sharon_ventura.sql`): `integration_health_checks` (connectorId/Name/Provider, status, message, durationMs, indexed on connectorId); `saved_reports` (name, type `"explorer"|"insight"`, provider, nullable `question` or nullable `connectorId`/`connectorName`/`tables[]` depending on type, `lastRunAt`, `lastResultSummary`); `roles` (name unique, description, color) and `role_permissions` (roleId FK cascade, `pageKey` text matching a nav entry's `title`, indexed on roleId). New services: `mediaSearchService.ts` (`searchMediaLibrary` — one query joining/aggregating `storageFiles`, `brandAssets`+`storageFiles`, and captioned `galleryImageMeta` rows; the page also calls the existing `listThemeGalleryFiles()` client-side to include gallery images with no caption yet, deduped by key); `integrationHealthService.ts` (`recordHealthCheck`/`listHealthChecks`/`clearHealthChecks`); `savedReportService.ts` (`listSavedReports`/`createSavedReport`/`deleteSavedReport`/`updateSavedReportRun`); `roleService.ts` (`listRoles` — joins `roles`+`role_permissions` into `RoleWithPermissions.pageKeys`, `createRole`/`deleteRole`/`setRolePermissions` — full-replace of a role's permission set per call, simplest way to sync a checkbox matrix). Integration Health reuses the exact `DISCOVERY_QUERIES`/`SELECT 1` test pattern already on `/connectors`. Saved Reports' "explorer" run type reproduces `/data-explorer`'s AI-generate-SQL-then-run flow inline (`buildSchemaContext()` + `runNaturalLanguageQuery`); its "insight" run type reproduces `/insights`' live-table-query-then-summarize flow inline (`useQuerySource` per selected table, then `useAiModel`) — both existing services were reused as-is, not modified. Roles & Permissions reuses the existing `flattenNavEntries(routes)` utility (from `src/components/layout/nav.ts`, already used by `GlobalSearch`/the sidebar's "Recently Visited") to enumerate every real nav page for the matrix, so it always reflects the current nav without a separate hardcoded page list.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; all four pages reuse `DATABASE_URL` (all four require `databaseEnabled`) and, for Media Library Search specifically, also `s3Enabled` (gated on both, since it searches S3-backed content). Saved Reports' "insight" type discovers a connector's tables via the same `DISCOVERY_QUERIES` allow-list already used on `/insights`/`/connectors`/`/data-dashboard` — a connector outside that allow-list simply shows no discoverable tables in the create-report dialog. New dialogs (Saved Reports' "New Saved Report", Roles & Permissions' "New Role") use the app's established conditional-mounting pattern (`{open && <DialogContent>...}`) to avoid the known Base UI dialog-won't-close issue documented in multiple entries above.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: User Roles & Permissions is explicitly non-enforcing — granting or revoking a page in the matrix has no effect on what any real user can actually access, since there is no custom login/session tied to these roles; it exists purely to document intended access for reference. Media Library Search's Gallery coverage only includes images that either have a caption (from `galleryImageMeta`) or are found in the live S3 listing at request time — there's no unified single query across all three sources' storage, so it's two calls merged client-side. Saved Reports' "insight" type re-runs against `SELECT * FROM "<table>" LIMIT 50` for every selected table on every run (no caching of the underlying row data, only the AI-generated summary is cached) — consistent with how `/insights` itself always re-queries live data rather than caching table rows. Integration Health's "testable" connector list is bounded by the same `DISCOVERY_QUERIES` allow-list as `/connectors`'s own Test button — connector types outside that allow-list (AI/ML models, non-SQL sources like Salesforce/QuickBooks, all destinations) don't appear on the page at all rather than showing a disabled/non-functional test button.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `integration_health_checks`, `saved_reports`, `roles`, `role_permissions` all in the table list, 35 tables total). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all eight created/touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new unused-import error). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on all created/touched files — no new lint issues beyond the same pre-existing `useExhaustiveDependencies` pattern already tolerated elsewhere in this app (e.g. `insights.tsx`), applied identically here with the same `eslint-disable-next-line` comment convention. Confirmed `routeTree.gen.ts` picked up all four new routes and that `/media-search`, `/integration-health`, `/saved-reports`, `/roles-permissions` all return 307 (auth redirect, no 500) from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry and a To-Do item documenting all four new pages, marked done.

### Fix: Saved Reports 422 on Supabase connector + "No tables discovered"

1. **Goal / behavior**: Two follow-up fixes to Saved Reports' "insight" type table picker/runner (from the entry above). (a) Running an insight report against a Supabase connector failed with `AIS API request failed with status 422` — the saved table list included Supabase's internal `auth` schema tables (`identities`, `flow_state`, etc.) captured without their schema, so the run-time query referenced a bare, unresolvable table name. (b) Separately, the table picker in the "New Saved Report" dialog then started showing "No tables discovered for this connector" even for connectors with real tables.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. (a) The create-dialog's table-discovery `onSuccess` now captures `table_schema` alongside `table_name` and stores the qualified `"schema.table"` string as each table's value (previously just the bare table name); a new `quoteIdentPart`/`qualifiedTableRef` helper in `saved-reports.tsx` quotes each segment of a saved table reference separately at run time (double quotes, or backticks for BigQuery, mirroring `/insights`' own `quoteIdent` pattern) instead of wrapping the whole possibly-qualified string in one pair of quotes. The one existing broken saved report row was repointed at valid `public` schema tables directly in the database. (b) Root cause of the picker regression: the discovery query used for populating the table list was the same column-per-row `DISCOVERY_QUERIES` query used elsewhere in the app — on a multi-schema database like Supabase, non-`public` schemas (`auth`, `storage`, `vault`, `realtime`) sort before `public` alphabetically and have many columns, so the platform's query response was very likely truncated before it ever reached the `public` schema's rows, leaving nothing behind after schema-scoping. Added a new, much lighter `TABLE_LIST_QUERIES` map (one row per *table*, not per column, via `information_schema.tables`/`all_tables`/BigQuery's own `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.TABLES` — scoped directly to `public`/current-database/current-user server-side) used in place of `DISCOVERY_QUERIES` for this page's table picker specifically, falling back to the old column-level query only for providers without a dedicated table-list query.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: `TABLE_LIST_QUERIES` only has dedicated entries for Postgresql/MariaDB/Oracle/BigQuery (the same engines with confirmed-working live discovery elsewhere in this app) — other providers fall back to the older, truncation-prone column-level query, same risk as before for those specific engines.
5. **Validation**: Live-verified both the 422 root cause and the fix directly via `aisquared_connectors_query_source` against the real Supabase connector (id 2108): confirmed `auth.identities`/`auth.flow_state`/etc. exist and a bare `SELECT * FROM "identities"` fails, confirmed the new schema-qualified quoted query succeeds, and confirmed the new lightweight `information_schema.tables` query returns exactly the 14 real `public` tables (`employees`, `departments`, etc.) with no truncation. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write`/`check` on the touched file — same pre-existing `useExhaustiveDependencies` pattern as before, not a new regression. Confirmed `/saved-reports` still returns 307 (auth redirect, no 500) from the dev server. Added two Changelog entries (v0.87.1, v0.87.2) and two To-Do items documenting both fixes, marked done.

### Performance pass: parallel table fetches, bounded Media Search queries, more cached list queries

1. **Goal / behavior**: A second performance pass across the app (following an earlier indexes/caching pass). Three changes: (a) Saved Reports' "insight" run type now fetches all of a report's selected tables in parallel instead of one at a time. (b) Media Library Search's three underlying database queries are now capped instead of unbounded. (c) Several remaining list/summary queries across the app gained a `staleTime` that were missed in the earlier caching pass.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. (a) `saved-reports.tsx`'s `handleRunInsight` replaced its sequential `for (const table of report.tables) { await ... }` loop with `Promise.all(report.tables.map(...))`, since each table's query is independent of the others. (b) `mediaSearchService.ts`'s three `db.select()` calls (`storageFiles`, `brandAssets` joined, `galleryImageMeta`) each gained `.limit(500)`, matching the existing capped-history pattern already used by `listHealthChecks`. (c) Added `staleTime` (15-30s) to: `analytics.tsx`'s summary/comparison queries, `audit.tsx`'s summary query, `error-log.tsx`'s summary query, `data-manager.tsx`'s reports query, `insights.tsx`'s AI Insights history query, `storage.tsx`'s duplicate-files query, `query.tsx`'s and `workflows.tsx`'s registry-sync-status queries, `workflows.tsx`'s workflow-runs query, and `index.tsx`'s quick-run workflow-picker query.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: `mediaSearchService`'s 500-row cap per source means a workspace with more than 500 files in any one of Storage/Brand Assets/Gallery-with-captions will only see its most recent 500 in search results — acceptable at this app's scale, same tradeoff already accepted for `listHealthChecks`'s 200-row cap. `roleService.ts`'s `listRoles` (also unbounded) was deliberately left as-is — roles and their permissions are inherently small (a handful of roles × the app's own page count), not a real growth risk.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new type error from a lost type-narrowing inside a `.map()` closure). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files. Confirmed `/saved-reports` still returns 307 (auth redirect, no 500) from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry and a To-Do item documenting the performance pass, marked done.

### Sidebar reorganization: regrouped nav sections, alphabetized, removed a duplicate Settings tab

1. **Goal / behavior**: Reorganized the sidebar navigation (grown to 30 pages over many feature additions) into more logical groups and alphabetized every section's items. `src/components/layout/index.ts`'s "Files & Media"/"AI & Content"/"Live Insights" groups were reshuffled: Document Pipeline and Knowledge Base moved out of Files & Media/Live Insights into AI & Content (they're AI-driven document tools, not plain file storage); the remaining "Live Insights" group was renamed **"Insights & Data"** (AI Insights, Data Dashboard, Data Explorer, Saved Reports); Files & Media kept the pure file/data-management pages (Brand & Asset Library, Data Manager, Export Center, Gallery, Import Wizard, Inventory, Media Library Search, Storage). Productivity, Platform, Monitoring & Logs, and Configuration keep their existing membership, just alphabetized. Also removed one confirmed real duplicate: Settings' "API Keys" tab, which managed the exact same `api_keys` table as the more full-featured standalone Secrets page (rotate/reveal/copy/expiry — the Settings tab only had list/create/delete) — the tab and its `ApiKeysPanel` usage were removed from `settings.tsx` so there's one canonical place (`/secrets`) to manage API keys; Webhooks/Integrations/Backups tabs on Settings are untouched (not duplicated elsewhere).
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change — pure nav-config and one-page markup edit. `src/components/layout/index.ts` — reordered/regrouped the `routes` array only (same `title`/`url`/`icon` values for every existing entry, no new pages, no deletions). `settings.tsx` — removed the `api-keys` `TabsTrigger` and its `TabsContent` block plus the now-unused `ApiKeysPanel` import; `ApiKeysPanel.tsx` itself (and the `api_keys` table/`configPanelService.ts` it's built on) was left in place, unused — the Secrets page (`apiKeyService.ts`, a separate service reusing the same `api_keys` table with additional columns) remains fully functional and is now the only UI surface for it.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This is a pure reorganization — no page was deleted, renamed, or had its URL changed, so no links/bookmarks break. `ApiKeysPanel.tsx`/`configPanelService.ts`'s API-key-specific procedures are now dead code (unused by any route) — left in place rather than deleted, matching this app's convention of leaving dormant features in place rather than reverting with more churn. Other overlapping-looking pages (AI Insights vs. Data Explorer vs. Saved Reports; Export Center vs. Data Manager) were reviewed and intentionally kept separate — each serves a genuinely different interaction model or environment (dev-only vs. published), confirmed with the user rather than assumed.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across both touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on both touched files. Confirmed `/settings`, `/`, and a spot-check of the reshuffled pages (`/document-pipeline`, `/knowledge-base`, `/data-dashboard`, `/insights`, `/data-explorer`, `/saved-reports`, `/brand-assets`, `/storage`) all still return 307 (auth redirect, no 500) from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry and a To-Do item documenting the reorganization, marked done.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.83.0, id 136) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Four new pages: Sentiment & Tone Analyzer, Content Moderation, Document Comparison, Prompt Library

1. **Goal / behavior**: Four new pages added to the AI & Content nav group. **Sentiment & Tone Analyzer** (`/sentiment-analyzer`) — paste text, pick an AI provider, get an overall sentiment (positive/negative/neutral + confidence), up to 5 tone descriptors, and up to 5 supporting key phrases; every run is saved to a history list (deletable). **Content Moderation** (`/content-moderation`) — paste text or load a document (same file types as AI Summariser: txt/md/csv/log/json/xml/html/rst/rtf/pdf/docx/odt) and get a flagged/clean verdict plus per-category severity (hate speech, harassment, violence, self-harm, sexual content, misinformation); every check is saved to a history list (deletable). **Document Comparison** (`/document-comparison`) — two paste-or-upload document slots, produces a line-level diff (added/removed counts + colored line view) plus an AI-generated plain-English bulleted summary of what changed; no history (not requested). **Prompt Library** (`/prompt-library`) — save/search/edit/delete reusable prompts (title, body, optional category, optional tags) with a Copy-to-clipboard button per card; store/copy only, no direct "send to Chat" integration (confirmed scope).
2. **Data flow**: New `sentiment_analyses`, `moderation_checks`, `saved_prompts` tables in `src/server/db/schema.ts`, migrated via `db:generate`/`db:migrate` (migration `0029_silent_marvex.sql`). New `src/services/db/sentimentAnalysisService.ts`, `moderationService.ts`, `promptLibraryService.ts` (`createServerFn` + `requireAppAccess()`, standard list/create/delete, plus update for prompts). Sentiment Analyzer and Content Moderation both reuse `useAiModel()`/`AiProviderSelect` (the shared Anthropic/OpenAI/AI Squared Bolt dispatcher already used by AI Summariser/Insights/etc.) and ask the model to return a strict JSON shape, parsed client-side via a small regex-extract-then-`JSON.parse` helper (falls back to showing the raw text if parsing fails, never throws). Content Moderation reuses the existing `extractUploadedDocumentText` server function (the same PDF/DOCX/ODT extractor AI Summariser uses) for file uploads. Document Comparison implements a small hand-rolled LCS-based line diff (`diffLines`, no external diff library) client-side, plus the same `useAiModel()` pattern for the change-summary text; no database table since history wasn't in scope. Prompt Library is a plain `useQuery`/`useMutation` CRUD page, dialogs using the app's established conditional-mounting pattern (`{dialogOpen && <Dialog>...}`) to avoid the known Base UI dialog-won't-close issue documented elsewhere in this file.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL` and the existing `OPENAI_API_KEY`/`ANTHROPIC_KEY` secrets / AI Squared Bolt connector already wired up for every other AI-powered page. All four pages registered in `src/components/layout/index.ts`'s "AI & Content" nav group, alphabetized alongside the existing four entries.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Sentiment/Moderation parsing depends on the model actually returning valid JSON in the requested shape — if it doesn't, the page falls back to showing the model's raw text response instead of structured fields (no retry/re-prompt loop). Content Moderation's category list is a fixed set of 6 categories requested in the prompt, not user-configurable. Document Comparison's line diff is a plain LCS algorithm (line-granularity, not word-level) — good for spotting added/removed/reordered lines, not fine-grained in-line word changes. Prompt Library has no "send directly to AI Chat" integration per the confirmed scope — only copy-to-clipboard.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `sentiment_analyses`, `moderation_checks`, `saved_prompts` in the table list). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all created/touched files. Confirmed `routeTree.gen.ts` picked up all four new routes. Added a Changelog entry (v2.84.0) and a To-Do item documenting the four new pages, marked done.

### Theme Generator: Extract Palette from Image, Chart Color Theming

1. **Goal / behavior**: Two additions to `/theme`. (a) A new "Extract from Image" button opens a dialog listing image assets from the Brand & Asset Library; picking one extracts its dominant colors client-side and auto-fills the builder's background gradient (2 colors), overlay tint, up to 4 accent colors, and a 5-color chart palette — a quick way to derive an on-brand theme from a logo/photo instead of picking every color by hand. (b) A new "Charts" tab lets a theme override the app's Recharts series colors (`--chart-1`..`--chart-5`, already read by every chart in the app — Analytics, Data Dashboard, Insights, etc.) with 5 custom colors and a live bar-chart preview, or stay on "App Default" to keep the existing indigo palette. Surprise Me and Design For Me were both extended to also randomize/generate a matching chart palette (about half the time for Surprise Me, tied to a new "Charts" checkbox for Design For Me).
2. **Data flow**: Added `custom_themes.chart_colors` (`text[]`, nullable — null/empty means "app default", same convention as `radius`/`badgeShape`/etc.) via migration `0030_wooden_sharon_ventura.sql`. `themeService.ts`'s `CustomThemeInput` gained `chartColors`, defaulted to `null` on insert. `ThemeApplier.tsx` now sets/clears `--chart-1`..`--chart-5` on `<html>` when the active theme has `chartColors` — since every chart component already reads these CSS variables (`var(--chart-1)` etc., confirmed via `analytics.tsx`/`data-dashboard.tsx`), no chart component itself needed to change. New `extractPaletteFromImage(dataUrl, count)` in `customTheme.ts` — a pure client-side canvas function: draws the image onto a downscaled (120x120) offscreen canvas, buckets pixels into a coarse 5-bit-per-channel RGB grid, averages each bucket's real pixel values, skips near-transparent/near-white/near-black pixels, and returns up to `count` hex colors ordered by frequency. New `src/services/db/themeImagePaletteService.ts`'s `getImageAsDataUrl` downloads an S3 object's bytes server-side (via the existing `downloadFile` S3 helper) and returns a base64 data URL — needed because the bucket has no CORS policy, so a browser can't safely `fetch()`/canvas-sample a presigned S3 URL directly (tainted canvas); a same-origin data URL sidesteps that. `theme.tsx`'s "Extract from Image" dialog calls `listBrandAssets()` (existing service) filtered to `contentType.startsWith("image/")`, renders thumbnails via a new `BrandAssetThumb` component (resolves a presigned URL per asset via the existing `getStorageFileUrl`), and on pick calls `getImageAsDataUrl` -> `extractPaletteFromImage` -> distributes the returned palette across `gradStart`/`gradEnd`/`overlayColor`/`accentColors`/`chartColors` state.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL` and the already-provisioned S3 storage. The image picker is gated on both `databaseEnabled` (to list Brand Assets) and `s3Enabled` (to resolve/download the actual image bytes) — the "Extract from Image" button is disabled with an explanatory tooltip if either is off.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Color extraction is a simple frequency-based bucket quantization (not a proper k-means/median-cut algorithm) — good enough for "on-brand accent colors from a logo," but can occasionally return several very similar shades if the image has a dominant near-uniform background; no dedup-by-similarity pass was added on top. The dark-mode variant fields (`darkBackground` etc.) are untouched by extraction — only the light-mode palette + chart colors are filled in. Chart color theming is a single fixed palette (not light/dark-variant-aware) — it does not change based on the theme's manual light/dark toggle, unlike background/overlay/accent colors.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `custom_themes` column count increased, no new tables). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new unused-import error). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched/created files. Confirmed `/theme` still compiles and the dev server serves it. Added a Changelog entry (v2.85.0) and a To-Do item documenting both features, marked done.

### Theme Generator: Extract from Image now also supports the Theme Gallery

1. **Goal / behavior**: The "Extract from Image" dialog on `/theme` (see the entry above) gained a source toggle — **Brand Assets** (original behavior) or **Theme Gallery** (the curated background art/GIF library already used elsewhere on this page for the Image/GIF background picker) — so a user can pull a color palette from either library, not just Brand Assets.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change. `theme.tsx` — new `extractSource: "brand" | "gallery"` state driving a `ToggleGroup` at the top of the dialog (same array-value pattern already used by the Background Type toggle elsewhere on this page). The Theme Gallery branch reuses the `galleryFiles` query (already fetched for the existing background-image picker, via `listThemeGalleryFiles()`) and renders each file through the same `BrandAssetThumb` presigned-URL-thumbnail component and `handleExtractFromImage()` handler used for Brand Assets — that handler was already generic over `{fileKey, contentType, name}`, so no changes were needed there; only the picker's data source and rendering branch are new.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. The dialog is gated on `s3Enabled` overall (needed for either source); the Brand Assets branch additionally requires `dbEnabled` (to list Brand Assets from the database), while the Theme Gallery branch does not (gallery files are read directly from S3, no DB row).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Same extraction-quality caveats as the original feature (simple frequency-based color quantization, not a proper clustering algorithm) — this change only adds a second image source, not a different extraction method.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.86.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Theme Generator: Simple/Advanced mode for non-technical users

1. **Goal / behavior**: `/theme`'s "Step 2 — Customize" card gained a **Simple / Advanced** toggle (defaulting to **Simple**). Simple mode only shows the Background and Effects tabs — Badges, Cards, Charts, Scope, and Advanced are hidden — alongside the existing guided actions (Surprise Me, Design For Me, Extract from Image) that already let a user get a complete theme without configuring anything manually. Advanced mode restores every tab exactly as before. A small "?" help icon (`HelpTip`) was added next to the "Background Type" control as a first example of clarifying a jargon-y label with a one-line plain-language explanation on hover/focus.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change — pure UI. `theme.tsx` — new `builderMode: "simple" | "advanced"` state and a `ToggleGroup` in the card header (same array-value pattern used elsewhere on this page) drives which `TabsTrigger`s render; the previously-uncontrolled `Tabs` (`defaultValue="background"`) became a controlled `Tabs value={activeBuilderTab} onValueChange={...}` so switching to Simple while a now-hidden tab (e.g. "Cards") is active can reset the selection back to "background" instead of showing a blank pane. New reusable `HelpTip({ text })` component (`Tooltip`/`TooltipTrigger`/`TooltipContent` from the already-globally-mounted `TooltipProvider` in `__root.tsx`) renders a small `HelpCircle` icon button; only wired up on Background Type so far as a proof of the pattern — the same component can be dropped next to any other control's `<Label>` later.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. `builderMode`/`activeBuilderTab` are component-local state (reset to Simple/Background on reload) — not persisted per-theme or per-user.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Only Background Type got a `HelpTip` in this pass — the other controls on this large page already have short helper `<p>` text under them (confirmed by reading the Effects tab), which was judged sufficient for now rather than adding a tooltip to every single field. Simple mode hides tabs entirely rather than disabling/graying them out — a Simple-mode user who previously set Badge/Card/Chart/Scope/Advanced fields (e.g. via Design For Me, which sets Cards/Badges/Charts even while in Simple mode) won't see those fields to edit until switching to Advanced, but the values themselves are preserved and still get saved.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Added a Changelog entry (v2.87.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Merged Data Dashboard into AI Insights as a "Chart View" tab; removed the standalone page

1. **Goal / behavior**: The standalone Data Dashboard page (`/data-dashboard`) is gone. Its auto-generated-charts feature (pick a table discovered live in a connector's schema, view row/column/average metric cards, a group-by bar chart, and a raw data table) is now a "Chart View" tab inside AI Insights (`/insights`), next to a new "Ask AI" tab that holds all of AI Insights' pre-existing behavior (Generate Insights / Ask a Question / Insight History) unchanged. Chart View reuses the same Data Source connector already selected at the top of the page — no separate connector picker.
2. **Data flow**: No schema change. New `src/components/insights/ChartView.tsx` — the table-discovery/row-fetch/chart-generation logic ported verbatim from the old `data-dashboard.tsx` (same `DISCOVERY_QUERIES`-based schema discovery, `quoteIdent`, `analyzeColumns` heuristic, Recharts bar chart, and results table), but as a presentational component taking `connectorId`/`connectorProvider`/`connectorName` as props instead of owning its own connector picker. `insights.tsx` — wrapped the existing "Tables to analyze" card, the inner Generate Insights/Ask a Question `Tabs`, and the Insight History card inside a new outer `Tabs` first tab ("Ask AI", default); added a second outer tab ("Chart View") rendering `<ChartView connectorId={connectorId} connectorProvider={connectorProvider} connectorName={connectorName} />`, reusing the page's existing connector-selection state as-is.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. `src/components/layout/index.ts` — removed the "Data Dashboard" nav entry (and its now-unused `LayoutPanelTop` icon import) from the Insights & Data group; `GlobalSearch.tsx`'s keyword map dropped the `/data-dashboard` entry and folded its keywords ("live data metrics", "charts") into the existing `/insights` entry so the page is still findable via search.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Chart View only ever charts whichever connector is currently selected in AI Insights' own Data Source picker (already filtered to data-only, SQL-queryable connectors) — there's no independent connector choice for charts vs. AI Q&A anymore, by design (the whole point of merging was one shared data-source selection). No behavior change to the charting logic itself — it's a straight port.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched/created files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched/created files. Confirmed no other code (besides the historical AGENTS.md log entries above, left as-is) still references `data-dashboard`.

### New page: Animated Theme Generator (`/animated-theme-generator`)

1. **Goal / behavior**: A new standalone page (AI & Content nav group) for generating and previewing animated gradient themes — separate from the main Theme Generator (`/theme`), which is an app-wide theming system. Pick a start/end color, gradient direction, one of 8 animation styles (Wave, Slow Zoom, Shimmer, Pulse, Floating Glow, Slow Drift, Aurora Shift, Prism Shift, reusing the CSS animation classes added for `/theme`), and a speed slider, or click Randomize for a fully random theme. A live preview shows the animated gradient. Themes can be saved by name and are listed in a grid below with Load (back into the preview) and Delete actions.
2. **Data flow**: New `animated_themes` table in `src/server/db/schema.ts` (name, gradStart/gradEnd/gradDirection, animation `$type<>()` union, speed, nullable accentColors array), migrated via `db:generate`/`db:migrate` (migration `0033_loving_shinobi_shaw.sql`) — deliberately a separate table from `custom_themes` per the user's explicit request for "a new database," since this page is a self-contained preview/collection tool, not wired into the app-wide `ThemeApplier`. New `src/services/db/animatedThemeService.ts` (`listAnimatedThemes`/`createAnimatedTheme`/`deleteAnimatedTheme`, standard `createServerFn` + `requireAppAccess()` pattern). `animated-theme-generator.tsx` reuses the existing `.theme-anim-*` CSS classes/keyframes (`src/styles.css`, originally built for `/theme`) for the live preview and saved-theme thumbnails, with animation duration driven by a local `animationDurationMs(speed)` helper (20s at speed 0 down to 3s at speed 100) applied via an inline `animationDuration` style rather than a new set of CSS variables.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL`. Registered in `src/components/layout/index.ts`'s "AI & Content" nav group (title "Animated Theme Generator", url `/animated-theme-generator`, icon `Sparkles`).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This page's saved themes are entirely separate from `/theme`'s `custom_themes` table and are never applied app-wide — Load only updates this page's own preview, matching the user's explicit choice of a standalone tool over extending the existing Theme Generator. Accent color swatches shown in the preview are decorative only (not applied to any UI element, unlike `/theme`'s accent-color system).
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `animated_themes` in the table list). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new type error from `Slider`'s `onValueChange` value possibly being a plain number instead of an array). `bunx biome format --write` on all created/touched files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.89.0) and a To-Do item documenting the new page, marked done.

### Three new, visually distinct animation styles: Rain Drops, Ripple Pulse, Snowfall

1. **Goal / behavior**: The existing animation styles (Wave/Zoom/Shimmer/Float/Drift/Aurora/Prism) all animate the base gradient itself (position/scale/opacity/filter), which can look fairly similar to each other. Added three new, more visually distinct styles to both `/theme` and `/animated-theme-generator`: **Rain Drops** (diagonal falling light-streak overlay), **Ripple Pulse** (expanding, fading concentric rings from the center, like a sonar ping), and **Snowfall** (three layers of falling dots at different sizes/speeds/depths).
2. **Data flow**: No migration needed — `animation` on both `custom_themes` and `animated_themes` is a plain `text()` column with a TypeScript `$type<>()` union (not a `pgEnum`), so widening it in `schema.ts`, `src/lib/customTheme.ts`'s `ThemeAnimation`, `src/services/db/themeService.ts`'s duplicate `ThemeAnimation`, and `src/services/db/animatedThemeService.ts`'s `AnimatedThemeAnimation` is type-level only. `src/styles.css` — the three new styles use a `::after` pseudo-element overlay (drawn via `repeating-linear-gradient`/`repeating-radial-gradient`/layered `radial-gradient`s) instead of animating the host element's own background, so the effect reads clearly regardless of the theme's chosen colors; the shared `.theme-anim-rain`/`.theme-anim-ripple`/`.theme-anim-snow` classes set their own `position: relative; overflow: hidden` so no consuming page needs extra wrapper classes. Every existing `.theme-anim-*` rule's hardcoded duration was also swapped for `var(--theme-anim-duration, <original value>)` — a no-op for existing callers (the variable is unset, so the fallback keeps the exact same speed as before) but lets a consumer that *does* set the CSS variable control every style uniformly. `animated-theme-generator.tsx`'s speed slider now sets `--theme-anim-duration` via inline `style` (previously it set `animationDuration` directly, which only worked for animations living on the host element itself, not the new pseudo-element-based ones) on both the live preview and each saved-theme thumbnail.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. `theme.tsx`'s `GALLERY_IMAGE_ANIMATIONS` (the auto-pick pool for theme-gallery images) gained `rain`/`snow` (compositor-cheap, background-position-only, same tier as the existing `wave`/`float`) but not `ripple` (uses `background-size`, kept out of the automatic pool though still manually selectable, same treatment as most non-filter animations). `handleSurpriseMe`/`handleDesignForMe` on `/theme`, and the `ANIMATIONS` array on `/animated-theme-generator`, all include the three new styles.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The three new styles don't respond to a theme's accent/overlay colors (their overlay pattern is always white-tinted, working over any base gradient) — unlike the base-gradient-animating styles, which are tinted by whatever colors the theme itself uses. `--theme-anim-duration` is currently only set by the Animated Theme Generator's speed slider; the main Theme Generator (`/theme`) and `ThemeApplier` don't expose a speed control, so their animations keep their original fixed hand-tuned durations.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files. `bun run db:status` — confirmed no schema drift (table list unchanged, as expected for a type-level-only column widening). Added a Changelog entry (v2.90.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Animated Theme Generator: per-animation color and direction controls

1. **Goal / behavior**: On `/animated-theme-generator`, the animation style can now have its own color and (for Rain Drops) a fall direction, independent from the base gradient's Start/End colors. A new "Animation Color" picker appears whenever Rain Drops, Ripple Pulse, or Snowfall is selected (the three overlay-based styles from the earlier entry above); a "Direction" slider (0-360°) appears additionally for Rain Drops only (Ripple expands from the center, Snow always falls straight down — direction has no effect for those). Both are saved per theme, reflected live in the preview and every saved-theme thumbnail, and included in the Randomize button's output.
2. **Data flow**: `animated_themes` gained `anim_color` (`text`, default `"#ffffff"`) and `anim_angle` (`integer`, default `100`) via migration `0034_warm_banshee.sql`. `animatedThemeService.ts`'s `AnimatedThemeInput` gained optional `animColor`/`animAngle` (defaulted server-side on insert). `styles.css` — the `.theme-anim-rain`/`.theme-anim-ripple`/`.theme-anim-snow` overlay rules (added in the entry above) now read `var(--theme-anim-color, white)` via `color-mix(in oklab, ...)` instead of a hardcoded white, and rain's `repeating-linear-gradient` angle reads `var(--theme-anim-angle, 100deg)` instead of a fixed `100deg` — both fall back to the exact original look when unset, so the main Theme Generator (`/theme`) and `ThemeApplier`, which don't set these variables, are unaffected. `animated-theme-generator.tsx` — new `animColor`/`animAngle` state, `showAnimColor`/`showAnimAngle` booleans gating the new controls, and both values are threaded through as `--theme-anim-color`/`--theme-anim-angle` inline CSS variables on the live preview div and each saved-theme thumbnail (same pattern as the existing `--theme-anim-duration` speed variable).
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL`.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The other 8 animation styles (Wave/Zoom/Shimmer/Pulse/Float/Drift/Aurora/Prism) ignore `animColor`/`animAngle` entirely — they animate the base gradient's own Start/End colors, which was an explicit prior design choice; adding independent color/direction controls for those would require also re-architecting them to draw a separate overlay layer, which was out of scope here (the user's request was answered by extending the three animations that already use a dedicated overlay).
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed no new tables, `animated_themes` column count increased). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files. Added a Changelog entry (v2.91.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Three more animation styles: Sparkle, Confetti, Orbit Glow

1. **Goal / behavior**: Added three more background animation styles to both `/theme` and `/animated-theme-generator`, on top of the six added in earlier entries above: **Sparkle** (three layers of twinkling dots fading in/out), **Confetti** (fast diagonal colorful flecks, similar to Rain Drops but shorter/denser and quicker), and **Orbit Glow** (a soft radial glow that moves in a circular path around the center via 5-step `background-position` keyframes). All three follow the same `::after`-overlay pattern as Rain/Ripple/Snow and support the Animation Color control; Confetti also supports the Direction slider (same as Rain Drops); Orbit and Sparkle don't expose a direction control since they have no meaningful directionality (centered / static-positioned respectively).
2. **Data flow**: No migration needed — same as the earlier animation-style additions, `animation` on both `custom_themes` and `animated_themes` is a plain `text()` column with a TypeScript union, so widening it in `schema.ts` (both tables), `src/lib/customTheme.ts`'s `ThemeAnimation`, `src/services/db/themeService.ts`'s duplicate `ThemeAnimation`, and `src/services/db/animatedThemeService.ts`'s `AnimatedThemeAnimation` is type-level only. `src/styles.css` — three new keyframe/overlay rules (`theme-anim-sparkle-twinkle`, `theme-anim-confetti-fall`, `theme-anim-orbit-move`), all reading `var(--theme-anim-color, white)`/`var(--theme-anim-duration, ...)` (Confetti also `var(--theme-anim-angle, 100deg)`) with the same fallback-preserves-original-look convention as every other animation variable added so far. `theme.tsx`'s `GALLERY_IMAGE_ANIMATIONS`, `handleSurpriseMe`'s pool, and `handleDesignForMe`'s curated pool all gained the three new values; `animated-theme-generator.tsx`'s `ANIMATIONS` picker array, `showAnimColor` (now an array-`includes` check covering all 6 overlay-based styles), and `showAnimAngle` (now `rain || confetti`) were updated accordingly.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Same as the prior overlay-style entries — these three don't respond to the theme's own gradient/accent colors (only to the new dedicated Animation Color control), and `--theme-anim-duration`/`--theme-anim-color`/`--theme-anim-angle` are only ever set by the Animated Theme Generator's controls; the main Theme Generator (`/theme`) and `ThemeApplier` don't expose Animation Color/Direction controls, so themes built there always get these three styles' default white color / default angle.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors across all touched files (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files. `bun run db:status` — confirmed no schema drift (table list unchanged, as expected for a type-level-only column widening). Added a Changelog entry (v2.92.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: Rain Drops, Confetti, Sparkle, and Orbit Glow looked too similar/static

1. **Goal / behavior**: Reworked the CSS for four of the overlay-based animation styles (shared by `/theme` and `/animated-theme-generator`) so each reads as a genuinely distinct effect. **Rain Drops** is now a two-layer parallax rainfall — a fast, sharper foreground layer of thin near-vertical streaks plus a slower, fainter background layer, both moving vertically at different speeds for depth — instead of a single diagonal streak pattern nearly identical to Confetti's. **Confetti** now uses two crossed sets of short diagonal lines forming small square flecks in two tones/sizes, descending in a zigzag (x oscillates while y increases via a 5-step keyframe) rather than falling in one straight diagonal line. **Sparkle** now has two independently-timed dot layers (`::before` + `::after`, offset by a negative `animation-delay`) each with a small position drift baked into their own keyframes, so different dots visibly twinkle and shift at different moments instead of the whole field fading in/out together (which read as static). **Orbit Glow**'s keyframes were rewritten from 4 corner stops (`20%/20%`, `80%/20%`, `80%/80%`, `20%/80%` — a rectangle) to 12 points sampled every 30° around an actual circle (`cos`/`sin` of the center at a fixed radius), so the glow visibly curves instead of tracing a rectangle's straight edges.
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change — purely `src/styles.css` keyframe/rule rewrites for `.theme-anim-rain`, `.theme-anim-confetti`, `.theme-anim-sparkle`, `.theme-anim-orbit` and their `::before`/`::after` overlays. All four still read `var(--theme-anim-color)`/`var(--theme-anim-duration)` (Rain/Confetti also `var(--theme-anim-angle)`) with the same fallback-preserves-a-sane-default convention as before — no changes needed in `customTheme.ts`, `schema.ts`, or either theme page's TypeScript, since the animation *names*/type unions are unchanged, only their visual implementation.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Confetti's "square fleck" look is still built from two crossed `repeating-linear-gradient` layers (a CSS-only overlay technique, like every other animation style here) rather than individually-rotating discrete shapes — it reads as scattered flecks at a glance but isn't a true particle simulation. Sparkle's two-layer twinkle timing is fixed (one layer delayed by 1/3 of the duration) rather than randomized per-dot, since CSS can't stagger individual dots within one repeating background pattern without more pseudo-elements. Orbit Glow's circular path is still a fixed-radius keyframe interpolation (12 straight-line segments approximating a circle, not true trigonometric easing) — smooth enough visually at typical durations but technically a many-sided polygon, not a mathematically perfect circle.
5. **Validation**: Verified the rewritten CSS's brace/paren balance programmatically (a quick Node script counting `{`/`}`/`(`/`)` across the whole file) since these are hand-written multi-layer gradients easy to mis-nest. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; no TypeScript was touched by this change). `bunx biome format --write` was attempted but Biome doesn't process `.css` files in this project's config (confirmed via its own "ignored path" message) — not applicable to this change. Added a Changelog entry (v2.93.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Rain Drops, Confetti, and Orbit Glow enhanced to match reference images

1. **Goal / behavior**: The user shared reference images for a rain-droplet-splash photo, a glowing concentric-ring "portal" GIF, and a multicolor confetti GIF, and asked the three matching animation styles to move closer to those references. **Rain Drops** gained a third streak layer of short, bright droplet "heads" (in addition to the existing two parallax streak layers) plus a new `::before` overlay of splash rings that pop and fade near the bottom edge at three positions, echoing the reference photo's mix of long streaks and visible droplet impacts. **Confetti** gained a third fleck layer whose color blends the theme's `--theme-anim-color` with a fixed magenta (`#ec4899`) tone (the existing second layer was similarly re-blended with the same magenta), plus a new violet (`#8b5cf6`)-blended third layer, so the field reads as genuinely multicolor pieces instead of one tint at different opacities, like the reference GIF's mixed pink/purple flecks. **Orbit Glow** was rebuilt entirely: instead of a single glow dot animating along a 12-point circular path, it's now two concentric ring outlines (`::after`, breathing in size/opacity together) plus a rotating diamond at the center (`::before`, a 45°-bordered square continuously spun via `transform: rotate()`), matching the reference GIF's glowing sci-fi-portal look.
2. **Data flow**: Purely `src/styles.css` changes — no schema/service/TypeScript changes, since the animation names/type unions are unchanged (only their visual implementation). `.theme-anim-rain` gained a `::before` overlay (in addition to its existing `::after`) for the splash rings; `.theme-anim-orbit` gained a `::before` overlay (a literal rotating square, not a background-image trick) for the diamond, positioned via `top/left: 50%` + `transform: translate(-50%, -50%) rotate(...)` rather than `inset: 0`, since it needs to stay a fixed small centered square while rotating. All still read `var(--theme-anim-color)`/`var(--theme-anim-duration)` (Rain also `var(--theme-anim-angle)`) with the same fallback-preserves-a-sane-default convention as every prior animation-style entry.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Confetti's magenta/violet accent hues are hardcoded (not user-configurable) — only the base tone comes from `--theme-anim-color`; a future ask for fully custom multi-color confetti would need new controls (e.g. a second/third `animColor`). Rain's splash rings all animate in the same phase (three positions, but one shared keyframe/timing) rather than fully independent random timing, which would need separate pseudo-elements per splash (only `::before`/`::after` are available per element). Orbit Glow's diamond rotation and ring pulse both read `--theme-anim-duration` but at different scaling (`/2` for the spin, `1×` for the ring pulse) — tuned by eye to look right together at the default duration, not derived from any formal ratio.
5. **Validation**: Verified brace/paren balance across the rewritten CSS via a quick Node script (counting `{`/`}`/`(`/`)`) since these are hand-written multi-layer gradients. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; no TypeScript touched). Biome does not process `.css` in this project. Added a Changelog entry (v2.94.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Fix: Rain Drops read as long lines sliding across, not falling droplets

1. **Goal / behavior**: The user reported Rain Drops' "lines are too long and just slide across the screen" and shared a rainfall reference GIF (short, separated droplets at multiple focal depths plus crown-shaped impact splashes). Rain is now built from many short discrete droplets falling at four different depths/speeds with a slight lean, and its impact splashes pop one at a time at four different spots across the lower half instead of all flashing simultaneously.
2. **Data flow**: Purely `src/styles.css` — no schema/service/TypeScript change (the animation name/type unions are untouched, only the visual implementation). **Root cause**: the droplet layers were drawn with `repeating-linear-gradient`, which by definition paints unbroken lines spanning the entire tile — so no amount of tuning stripe widths/angles could ever produce separated drops; it could only read as continuous streaks. `.theme-anim-rain::after` was rewritten to use four layers, each a *single* small elongated `radial-gradient` ellipse positioned inside its own tile (`background-size`), so tiling yields a field of short, separated drops; the four layers differ in tile size, drop length, brightness, and per-cycle travel distance (each an exact whole number of its own tile heights, keeping the loop seamless while every layer moves at a different speed for parallax). The fall angle moved from the gradient's own angle to `transform: skewX(calc(90deg - var(--theme-anim-angle, 95deg)))` (an ellipse can't be rotated in-place), with the overlay `inset` pushed past the element's edges so the skew never exposes a bare corner. `.theme-anim-rain::before` (splashes) went from 3 plain ring outlines sharing one pop-and-fade to 4 burst gradients (bright center core + expanding ring in one gradient) spread across the lower half, each growing within its own quarter of `theme-anim-rain-splash` while the shared opacity envelope returns to 0 exactly at each quarter boundary — so only one splash is ever visible at a time and each appears to grow and fade independently.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Still reads `--theme-anim-color`/`--theme-anim-angle`/`--theme-anim-duration` with the same fallback-preserves-a-sane-default convention as every other animation style (the fall animation now runs at `duration * 1.6` and the splash cycle at `duration * 4`, tuned so drops read as rain rather than a blur at the default speed).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Only two pseudo-elements exist per element, so all four droplet depths share one `::after` (one animation timeline — their differing speeds come from different per-layer travel distances within that one timeline, not independent timings) and all four splashes share one `::before`. Splash timing is therefore a fixed round-robin of four positions rather than randomized per impact, and splashes are not spatially correlated with where any particular droplet actually lands — a true particle simulation isn't achievable with CSS-only background layers.
5. **Validation**: Verified brace/paren balance across the rewritten CSS via the same Node/Bun counting script used in prior CSS-only entries (114/114 braces, 325/325 parens). Confirmed the file actually compiles through Tailwind/Vite rather than just reading correctly — fetched the dev server's compiled `src/styles.css` and grepped it for the new `theme-anim-rain-fall` keyframes, the new droplet gradient (`1.1px 11px at 22% 14%`), and the `skewX(calc(90deg ...))` transform, all present. No TypeScript was touched; Biome doesn't process `.css` in this project. Added a Changelog entry (v2.97.0) and a To-Do item documenting the fix, marked done.

### Removed Confetti and Orbit Glow animations; improved Sparkle twinkle timing

1. **Goal / behavior**: Per user request, the **Confetti** and **Orbit Glow** background animation styles (added/refined in the several entries above) were removed entirely from both `/theme` and `/animated-theme-generator` — they no longer appear in either animation picker, Surprise Me's random pool, or Design For Me's curated pool. **Sparkle** (kept) was reworked so its two dot layers dim from low to full opacity on more clearly different intervals — one layer runs at the base duration, the other at 1.4x with a negative delay — so individual sparkles visibly twinkle out of sync with each other instead of in lockstep.
2. **Data flow**: `"confetti"`/`"orbit"` were removed from the `ThemeAnimation`/`AnimatedThemeAnimation` type unions in `src/lib/customTheme.ts`, `src/services/db/themeService.ts`, `src/services/db/animatedThemeService.ts`, and both `animation` column `$type<>()` unions in `src/server/db/schema.ts` (type-level only, no migration needed — same convention as every other animation-style addition/removal in this file's history). `src/styles.css` — deleted the `.theme-anim-confetti`/`.theme-anim-orbit` rules and their `@keyframes` entirely; `.theme-anim-sparkle`'s `::after`/`::before` layers now use distinct opacity ranges and durations (`::before` runs at `calc(var(--theme-anim-duration) * 1.4)` instead of the same duration as `::after`), keeping the existing negative `animation-delay` offset. `theme.tsx` — removed `"confetti"`/`"orbit"` from `GALLERY_IMAGE_ANIMATIONS`, `handleSurpriseMe`'s pool, and `handleDesignForMe`'s curated pool. `animated-theme-generator.tsx` — removed both entries from the `ANIMATIONS` picker array; `showAnimColor` narrowed to `rain`/`ripple`/`snow`/`sparkle`; `showAnimAngle` narrowed to `rain` only (Confetti was the other style using the angle control).
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: Any theme previously saved with `animation: "confetti"` or `"orbit"` will fall back to rendering no animation overlay at all (the CSS class no longer exists) rather than erroring, since the column is a plain `text()` with no DB-level constraint — no data migration was run to reassign those rows since none were known to exist.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched TypeScript files (Biome doesn't process `.css` in this project). Confirmed no remaining `confetti`/`orbit` references anywhere in `src/` via a full grep. Added a Changelog entry (v2.96.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### New page: Chat Log (`/chat-log`)

1. **Goal / behavior**: A new page (Monitoring & Logs nav group) that records what the user has asked the AppGen assistant to do in this chat, a short plain-language summary of what the assistant did in response, and when — automatically, after every request going forward (per the user's explicit choice). Entries render newest-first as cards (timestamp, query, response summary) with a per-entry delete button.
2. **Data flow**: New `agent_chat_logs` table in `src/server/db/schema.ts` (`userQuery`, `responseSummary`, `createdAt`), migrated via `db:generate`/`db:migrate` (migration `0036_gray_bill_hollister.sql`) — deliberately separate from the pre-existing `chat_conversations`/`chat_messages` tables, which back the in-app `<ChatAssistant/>` feature, not this builder chat. New `src/services/db/agentChatLogService.ts` (`listAgentChatLogs`/`createAgentChatLog`/`deleteAgentChatLog`, standard `createServerFn` + `requireAppAccess()` pattern). `chat-log.tsx` is a plain `useQuery`/`useMutation` list page gated on `useCapabilities().databaseEnabled`, with loading/empty/not-configured states.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config; reuses `DATABASE_URL`. There is no automatic hook into this chat's raw messages from inside the app's own code — the agent itself calls `createAgentChatLog` (via a short one-off script, same pattern used elsewhere in this app's history for one-time data operations) after each turn in this chat, going forward, per the user's explicit choice ("log every turn automatically"). Two entries were backfilled for this session's immediately-preceding theme-background fix requests, per the user's explicit choice to backfill this session (but not earlier sessions).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This is not a live transcript capture — the agent must actively record each entry; if a future agent session forgets to call `createAgentChatLog`, that turn simply won't appear here. The "response summary" is a short paraphrase written by the agent, not the verbatim response text.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `agent_chat_logs` in the table list). `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all created/touched files. Confirmed `routeTree.gen.ts` picked up the new route and `/chat-log` returns 307 (auth redirect, no 500) from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry and a To-Do item documenting the new page, marked done.

### Chat Log: added session id

1. **Goal / behavior**: Each Chat Log entry (see the page above) now shows a "Session: <id>" badge. Since the platform exposes no true session identifier to this app's own code, the agent generates one id per continuous conversation and reuses it for every entry logged during that same session, so entries from one conversation are visually grouped.
2. **Data flow**: Added `agent_chat_logs.session_id` (`text`, NOT NULL) via migration `0037_cuddly_jimmy_woo.sql`. `CreateAgentChatLogInput` (`agentChatLogService.ts`) gained a required `sessionId` field. `chat-log.tsx` renders it as an outline `Badge` next to the timestamp.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Existing rows were backfilled with the literal string `"legacy-session"` before the column was made NOT NULL.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: The Neon HTTP driver cannot run multiple SQL statements in a single migration file without explicit `--> statement-breakpoint` markers between them — the first attempt at this migration (`ADD COLUMN` + `UPDATE` + `ALTER ... SET NOT NULL` with no breakpoints) failed with a syntax error (`42601`) and had to be rewritten with breakpoints before `db:migrate` succeeded. Session ids are agent-assigned, not cryptographically verified — they're just a grouping label.
5. **Validation**: `bun run db:generate` + fixed migration + `bun run db:migrate` + `bun run db:status` (confirmed `agent_chat_logs` column count increased, no new tables) — also directly queried the table to confirm the two pre-existing rows survived the migration with `session_id = "legacy-session"`. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only). `bunx biome format --write` on all touched files. Confirmed `/chat-log` still returns 307 (auth redirect, no 500) from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Orbit Glow: added neon glow + faint grid backdrop (still a CSS-only approximation)

1. **Goal / behavior**: Pushed Orbit Glow further toward the reference GIF: its two concentric rings and center diamond are now thicker and glow (`filter: drop-shadow(...)` in the theme's animation color), and a faint diagonal crosshatch grid (two low-opacity `repeating-linear-gradient` layers at ±45°) was added behind the rings, echoing the reference's plaid-like backdrop.
2. **Data flow**: Purely `src/styles.css` — `.theme-anim-orbit::after` gained two additional static crosshatch background layers (fixed `26px 26px` tile size, explicitly listed at every step of `theme-anim-orbit-rings` so CSS's cyclic background-shorthand behavior doesn't also animate their size) plus a `filter: drop-shadow(...)` for the glow; `.theme-anim-orbit::before` (the diamond) got a thicker `2px` border, a subtle two-stop fill gradient, `border-radius: 8px`, and its own `drop-shadow`.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config.
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: This remains a CSS gradient/keyframe effect, not a rendered/imported graphic — it cannot reproduce the reference GIF's exact hexagonal shape, inner ripple rings, or pixel-level glow falloff. Told the user directly that true pixel-for-pixel fidelity to a photo/GIF reference isn't achievable with CSS-only backgrounds, and that using a real uploaded image/GIF asset as the background (with only simple CSS motion layered on top, e.g. a slow zoom) would be the more faithful alternative if closer matching is needed.
5. **Validation**: Verified brace/paren balance via the same Node script used in prior CSS-only entries. `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; no TypeScript touched). Added a Changelog entry (v2.95.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done.

### Chat Log: search/filter, session grouping, CSV export, stats, pagination

1. **Goal / behavior**: The Chat Log page (`/chat-log`) gained: a search box matching query/summary/session id text; From/To date filters; entries grouped into collapsible sections per chat session (newest session's latest activity first, count badge per session); an "Export CSV" button that downloads the currently filtered entries; three summary stat cards (total entries, distinct sessions, most active day) computed over the filtered set; and Previous/Next pagination (10 session groups per page).
2. **Data flow**: No schema/service change — all client-side derived state in `chat-log.tsx` on top of the existing `listAgentChatLogs()` query. `filteredEntries` (search + date-range `useMemo`) feeds both the stats block and a `sessionGroups` `useMemo` (entries bucketed by `sessionId`, each group's `latestAt` = max `createdAt` among its entries, groups sorted by `latestAt` descending). Pagination slices `sessionGroups` (`PAGE_SIZE = 10`); each visible group renders inside a `Collapsible` (open/closed tracked per-session in a local `Set` state, collapsed by default) showing its entries as the same card layout used before this change. "Export CSV" calls the existing shared `downloadCsv()` (`@/lib/csvExport`) against `filteredEntries` (id/session/timestamp/query/summary columns) — no new server endpoint.
3. **Inputs/config**: No new env/config. Search/date filters and pagination reset to page 1 on any filter change; open/closed session state and filters are component-local (reset on reload), matching this app's convention for other local-only list-view UI state (e.g. Query page's Recent tab).
4. **Constraints / known limitations**: All entries are still fetched in one request (`listAgentChatLogs()` has no server-side pagination) — pagination here only controls what's rendered, same tradeoff already accepted for Audit Log/Error Log's own client-side pagination. "Most active day" buckets by the entry's local calendar day (from `createdAt`), not a specific timezone-normalized boundary.
5. **Validation**: `bunx tsc --noEmit -p .` — no new errors in the touched file (same pre-existing unrelated `ve.vite.config.ts` error only; fixed one real new type error passing a `number` timestamp where `formatTimestamp` expected `string | Date`). `bunx biome format --write` on the touched file. Confirmed `/chat-log` still returns 307 (auth redirect, no 500) from the dev server. Added a Changelog entry (v2.98.0) and a To-Do item documenting the change, marked done, and logged this turn to the Chat Log itself.
